## Summary
- 27 Jan 1562/3: 32/32 read, word-for-word agreement with Forbes II 315-318.
- 13 Apr 1563: 12/12 read, agrees with Forbes II 384-386 (passage 7 first word uncertain).
- 27 July 1563 (to the Queen): 2 passages, read in part ("my Lord of Warwick … be able to keep … men he hath");
  fits CSP vi no. 1078 (dated 28 July).
- 25 Jan 1563/4: long block read in large part; agrees with CSP vii no. 103; last line tentative.
- 22 Mar 1566/7 (= 22 Mar 1566, CSP viii no. 208): 27 passages, about 12 firm, the rest uncertain (short code
  groups like 1, 2, 4, 10, 11, 15, 20, 25-26 unread).
- 29 Apr 1566: 5/5 read, agrees with CSP viii no. 327.
- The key works as summarized in KEY.md; scribal slips seen: 5. for 5: (v), 4 for L (e), ⊔ for π in a few words.
- The reading was done from the glyphs; the cribs confirmed it but I did not write in crib words that the glyphs
  do not support. Where a word appears only because of the crib it is marked [?].

# No. 06, Smith to Queen Elizabeth, Valmont, 27/28 July 1563 — English

My Lord of Warwick (commanding the English garrison in Newhaven, Le Havre).

Whether he is able to hold it or not, [?nor] for how long, nor how many men he has in it.

No. 07, Smith to Cecil, Paris, 25 Jan 1563/4 (R9250, f. 159) decoded, not read

Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Paris, 25 Jan 1563/4: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (large part; checked against CSP F. 7 no. 103). In decipher_A.md, Letter 4: glyphs and plaintext per passage.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

Transcription

decipher_A.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136 — decipherment A

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

Key: KEY.md / decode/IMG_R9261_I43290_P1.jpg. Glyph transcription is normalized: digits with `.`/`:` as written,
`⊢̈` = m, `{`/`}` = l, `ξ` = d, `L` = e, `⊔ ⊓` = a, `γ ∝ α` = c, `π` = i, `X` = g, `ω` = h, `□` = f, `‡` = b,
`◇—◇` = w; code words given as `sign(word)`. Clear-English nulls omitted. [?] = uncertain letter/word,
⟨code⟩ = unread sign.

Read in large part; everything read agrees with CSP no. 103 (ladders at Étaples/Boulogne, surprise of a town in

## Summary
- 27 Jan 1562/3: 32/32 read, word-for-word agreement with Forbes II 315-318.
- 13 Apr 1563: 12/12 read, agrees with Forbes II 384-386 (passage 7 first word uncertain).
- 27 July 1563 (to the Queen): 2 passages, read in part ("my Lord of Warwick … be able to keep … men he hath");
  fits CSP vi no. 1078 (dated 28 July).
- 25 Jan 1563/4: long block read in large part; agrees with CSP vii no. 103; last line tentative.
- 22 Mar 1566/7 (= 22 Mar 1566, CSP viii no. 208): 27 passages, about 12 firm, the rest uncertain (short code
  groups like 1, 2, 4, 10, 11, 15, 20, 25-26 unread).
- 29 Apr 1566: 5/5 read, agrees with CSP viii no. 327.
- The key works as summarized in KEY.md; scribal slips seen: 5. for 5: (v), 4 for L (e), ⊔ for π in a few words.
- The reading was done from the glyphs; the cribs confirmed it but I did not write in crib words that the glyphs
  do not support. Where a word appears only because of the crib it is marked [?].
Decryption

decipher_A.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136 — decipherment A

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

Key: KEY.md / decode/IMG_R9261_I43290_P1.jpg. Glyph transcription is normalized: digits with `.`/`:` as written,
`⊢̈` = m, `{`/`}` = l, `ξ` = d, `L` = e, `⊔ ⊓` = a, `γ ∝ α` = c, `π` = i, `X` = g, `ω` = h, `□` = f, `‡` = b,
`◇—◇` = w; code words given as `sign(word)`. Clear-English nulls omitted. [?] = uncertain letter/word,
⟨code⟩ = unread sign.

DECODE R9250 P1 (long block), Add MS 4136 f.159. CSP Foreign vol. 7 no. 103 ("Orig., portions in cipher

  `‡5:4: T̈ ⊽⊔5:4⊔4 2.5:πL4:[?]` "but to live [in quiet]" `·A T̈ ⊢̈⊓θL ω⊔⊢̈ 9.L⊽□ 64 R:` "and to make himself rich"

Read in large part; everything read agrees with CSP no. 103 (ladders at Étaples/Boulogne, surprise of a town in
England or the Low Countries, no league with the Pope, nothing against the Queen of Navarre, "make himself rich").
The final clause about the father's treasure is not in CSP's summary and is tentative.

## Summary
- 27 Jan 1562/3: 32/32 read, word-for-word agreement with Forbes II 315-318.
- 13 Apr 1563: 12/12 read, agrees with Forbes II 384-386 (passage 7 first word uncertain).
- 27 July 1563 (to the Queen): 2 passages, read in part ("my Lord of Warwick … be able to keep … men he hath");
  fits CSP vi no. 1078 (dated 28 July).
- 25 Jan 1563/4: long block read in large part; agrees with CSP vii no. 103; last line tentative.
- 22 Mar 1566/7 (= 22 Mar 1566, CSP viii no. 208): 27 passages, about 12 firm, the rest uncertain (short code
  groups like 1, 2, 4, 10, 11, 15, 20, 25-26 unread).
- 29 Apr 1566: 5/5 read, agrees with CSP viii no. 327.
- The key works as summarized in KEY.md; scribal slips seen: 5. for 5: (v), 4 for L (e), ⊔ for π in a few words.
- The reading was done from the glyphs; the cribs confirmed it but I did not write in crib words that the glyphs
  do not support. Where a word appears only because of the crib it is marked [?].

no07_reading.md

<!-- Reading of no. 07, built 23 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (verification 3) from decipher_A.md Letter 4: the long cipher block of Add MS 4136 f. 159 (DECODE R9250 p. 1), read with Smith's key R9261. Forbes copied only the cipher; the clear text of the letter is not in print. The calendar, CSP Foreign vii no. 103 ("Orig., portions in cipher deciphered"), paraphrases the same passage and agrees with every word read: "The Ambassador of Spain sends him word that he has good advertisement that the French make ladders and other such equipages at Etaples and Boulogne. Whether they mean to surprise some town in England or attempt some place in the Low Country ... the King of Spain has entered into no such league with the Pope, or other Princes, as France bruits, nor ever will; and that he minds nothing against the Queen of Navarre, but to live in peace, and make himself rich." Marks: [?word] = carried only in part by its signs; [word] = supplied, not carried by the signs (the calendar has it); […] = signs not read. The spelling is the decipherment's. -->

# No. 07, Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Paris, 25 January 1563/4

## Reading

[?Not] only the [?French] […]. The ambassador of Spain send[s] me word that he hath good advertisement that the French
make ladders and other such equipages at Estaples and Boulogne, whether they mean to surprise [?some] town [?in]
England […] [or] [attempt] [some] [place] [in] the Low Countries. […] And he saith that the King of Spain [hath]
entered into no such league as France doth bruit with the Pope or other princes, nor never will, and that he mindeth
nothing against the queen of Navarre but to live [in] [quiet], and to make himself rich, and recover that treasure
which his father had s[pent] without […].

No. 08, Smith to Cecil, Valence, 1 Sep 1564 (R9252, f. 164)

Smith to Cecil, Valence, 1 Sep 1564: the Scots-French practice against the amity of England and Scotland; the Lord of Murray and the lords favouring religion; Huntly; the bishops and the Pope's council; Lethington, his brother at Bruges, the Bishop of Glasgow and a French pension; the Queen of Scots and Lord Robert; the old Pope; the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots (CSP vii 642 §3).

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

| 11 | ẘ ẏ .ſ F ξ6‡‡{L 1 ·ꝺ ⊔□6·⊔πLξ 57· ꝏ: 3:6·8:8{L | whom you shall find double, I am afraid, or rather triple | ✓ |
| 12 | .h da C· ·A 8L2γπ6310· ·o A: 2:ω67·L 86·π2γL10· | having ⟨C·⟩ and pencions of all three princes | "having pensions of all three princes" ✓ |

Read almost entirely. A few code signs are open (lines 4, 6, 7). The reading agrees with CSP no. 815 throughout.

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.
Decryption

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

no08_reading.md

<!-- Reading of no. 08, built 22 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (batch b03). Forbes copied only the 31 cipher stretches of this letter (Add MS 4136 f. 164, DECODE R9252 p. 2); the clear text is not in print. Every passage re-read on the image with Smith's key R9261 (decipher_D.md D1, corrected; no01-no23_reread_log.md). Each line below is one passage or a run of consecutive passages; the calendar (CSP Foreign vii no. 642 §3) shows how they sit in the letter: "a straighter alliance betwixt Scotland and France, and to break the amity betwixt England and Scotland ... the Earl of Murray and such lords and great men as are the great favourers of religion there ... to do some great enterprise that way ... These bishops here, and the Pope's council ... are as busy as may be ... Lethington, whose brother is at Bruges ... the Bishop of Glasgow ... a pension promised to Lethington by the French King ... of the King of Spain ... the Queen of Scotland shall be married to Lord Robert ... from England and Scotland ... to reduce the realm to the old Pope ... the love betwixt England and Scotland ... give Murray warning ... the Queen of Spain ... married already the King of Spain to the Queen of Scotland." [?] marks a word only partly carried; […] an unread sign. Spelling (v6, 23 Oct 2026): the figures', as Smith's key spells them (no01-no23_reread_log.md, decipher_*.md, no01-no23_corrections.tsv); the modernised spelling of 22 Oct restored by hand where the figures spell a word (code signs for names, places and titles carry no spelling and keep the key's gloss). No word was changed. Modern spelling is only in the English file. -->

# No. 08, Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Valence, 1 September 1564

Scotland; […]; Scotland. The amity; England; Scotland.

[The] Lord of Murray; such lords and great men; be the great favourers of religion there.

To do [?some] great enterprise that wise. The Scots […]; of the religion; for [?my] […] Lord of Murray.

[?The Earl of] Huntly died, and his sonnes were taken.

Bishops here; the Pope's counsel; be as busy as may be.

The Lord of Lethington; is at Bruges; the Bishop of Glasgow; pension; […] Lord of Lethington, of the French King; of
the King of Spain.

The Queen of Scots; to [?marry] Lord Robert. England and Scotland. […] to the old Pope. England and Scotland.

[?My] Lord of Murray. Of the Queen of Spain. The King of Spain to the Queen of Scots.

<!-- English of no08_reading.md. The passages are the ciphered stretches only; CSP Foreign vii no. 642 §3 gives their setting (see the reading's header). […] = unread sign. -->

# No. 08, Smith to Cecil, Valence, 1 September 1564 — English

Scotland; […]; Scotland. The amity; England; Scotland.

[The] Lord of Murray (James Stewart, Earl of Moray); such lords and great men; [who] are the great supporters of the
(Protestant) religion there.

To do [?some] great enterprise that way. The Scots […]; of the religion; for [?my] […] Lord of Murray.

[?The Earl of] Huntly died, and his sons were taken.

Bishops here; the Pope's council; are as busy as can be.

The Lord of Lethington (Maitland); is at Bruges; the Bishop of Glasgow; pension; […] Lord of Lethington, from the French
King; from the King of Spain.

The Queen of Scots; to [?marry] Lord Robert (Dudley). England and Scotland. […] to the old Pope. England and Scotland.

[?My] Lord of Murray. Of the Queen of Spain. The King of Spain to the Queen of Scots.

No. 09, Smith to Cecil, Valence, 3 Sep 1564 (R9252, f. 164) not prose

Smith to Cecil, Valence, 3 Sep 1564: Ninian Cockburn may come into England with Villeroy (CSP vii 650).

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.
Decryption

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.
Reading

# No. 09, Smith to Cecil, Valence, 3 September 1564 — English

Ninian Cockburn (the Scottish soldier in French service). Villeroy (Nicolas de Neufville, seigneur de Villeroy).

No. 10, Smith to Cecil, Avignon, 4 Oct 1564 (R9252, ff. 164-5) decoded, not read

Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Avignon, 4 Oct 1564: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (about 50 of 62 passages; checked against CSP F. 7 no. 714). Among them: "they feare that she is enclined to this last offer of Lenox". In decipher_C.md, the whole file: glyphs and plaintext per passage.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

Transcription

decipher_C.md

# Smith cipher passages, BL Add MS 4136: decipherment C

## Smith to Cecil, Avignon, 4 Oct 1564 (Forbes heading read so far as "27 Oct"): R9252 p.2 foot and p.3, Add MS 4136 ff.164-165

**Date.** The heading reads "from Avignon, 4 Oct. 1564". The figure is the same looped 4 as the 4 in "1564"; it is not "27".
CSP Foreign vol. 7 has no Smith letter of 27 Oct. It does have **no. 714, Smith to Cecil, Avignon, 1 Oct. 1564, P.S.
4 Oct.** ("Orig., portions in cipher, deciphered"; pp. 217-218). Every firm reading below follows no. 714 §2 in
order, so this is that letter. Crib saved in `csp/v7_pp215-233.html`.

Transcription conventions follow decipher_A.md. Clear nulls (Hull, Calais, Sandwich, Jersey, Antwerpe, bycause) are
dropped. Glyph notes for this letter:
- `ϕ` (a looped kite with a stem) = w (2:ϕ6 = two, three times).
- `{{` / `ſſ` / `ꟿ` = st (papistry, state, stronger, strong).
- `ʋ` (a small open v) = l (litle, gospel, Lenox).
- `ζ` variants (a T with a hook): `ζ` + dot = here (1, 24, 31); `ζ̤` = his/they/them (see each row); `ζ̈` = they/this.
  These dot placements are not stable in Forbes's copy, so they are read from context. Such rows are marked [?].
- `J̣` = is/are; `!` = in/into; `凸`-variant = Earl (before Murray, Lennox, Warwick; plain 凸 = England in 14).
- `B:` = Bishop; `C̄` = Congregation; `M̅` = marriage (33, 41, 48); `ṃ` = marry; `Ṗ` privy, `.P` practise.

## Summary
- Letter identified: the heading's date is **4 Oct 1564**, not 27 Oct; it is CSP Foreign vii **no. 714** (Avignon,
  1 Oct with P.S. 4 Oct 1564, "portions in cipher, deciphered"). The ciphered passages carry §2 of the calendar
  entry in order, from "Their designs here are two" to "this last offer of Lennox's".
- 62 numbered passages (there is no (6)), plus an unnumbered line (12) and a run-on (29b).
  - About 50 read firmly: 1-4, 7, 9-11, 13-16, 19-21, 24-29b, 31-33, 35-38, 40-51, 53-57, 59-63.
  - About 10 read in part or uncertain: 5, 8, 12, 17, 18, 22, 23, 30, 34, 39, 52, 58.
  - 22 and 23 ("by little and little and by … great") have no counterpart in the CSP summary and are only partly read.
- Code words used here beyond decipher_A: ϕ = w; ſſ/ꟿ = st; ʋ = l; J̣ = is/are; ! = in/into. The ζ family
  (here/his/they/this/thing) and the Earl sign carry the most doubt, because Forbes's dots are not consistent.
- The crib was used only to check readings. The spelled-out words (designs, papistry, gospel, Italian, contreband,
  interrupt, Glasgow, embassador, profession, Poictiers, bushoprick, reserved, dispatcheth, espials, negligent,
  anker, Robert, cardinals, Guise, stronger, Babtista, London, dispache, enclined, Lenox) come from the glyphs alone.

# Smith cipher passages, BL Add MS 4136: decipherment C

## Smith to Cecil, Avignon, 4 Oct 1564 (Forbes heading read so far as "27 Oct"): R9252 p.2 foot and p.3, Add MS 4136 ff.164-165

**Date.** The heading reads "from Avignon, 4 Oct. 1564". The figure is the same looped 4 as the 4 in "1564"; it is not "27".
CSP Foreign vol. 7 has no Smith letter of 27 Oct. It does have **no. 714, Smith to Cecil, Avignon, 1 Oct. 1564, P.S.
4 Oct.** ("Orig., portions in cipher, deciphered"; pp. 217-218). Every firm reading below follows no. 714 §2 in
order, so this is that letter. Crib saved in `csp/v7_pp215-233.html`.

Transcription conventions follow decipher_A.md. Clear nulls (Hull, Calais, Sandwich, Jersey, Antwerpe, bycause) are
dropped. Glyph notes for this letter:
- `ϕ` (a looped kite with a stem) = w (2:ϕ6 = two, three times).
- `{{` / `ſſ` / `ꟿ` = st (papistry, state, stronger, strong).
- `ʋ` (a small open v) = l (litle, gospel, Lenox).
- `ζ` variants (a T with a hook): `ζ` + dot = here (1, 24, 31); `ζ̤` = his/they/them (see each row); `ζ̈` = they/this.
  These dot placements are not stable in Forbes's copy, so they are read from context. Such rows are marked [?].
- `J̣` = is/are; `!` = in/into; `凸`-variant = Earl (before Murray, Lennox, Warwick; plain 凸 = England in 14).
- `B:` = Bishop; `C̄` = Congregation; `M̅` = marriage (33, 41, 48); `ṃ` = marry; `Ṗ` privy, `.P` practise.

<!-- Reading of no. 10, built 23 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (verification 3) from decipher_C.md: the 62 numbered cipher passages of Add MS 4136 ff. 164-165 (DECODE R9252 pp. 2-3), read with Smith's key R9261. Forbes copied only the cipher; the clear text of the letter is not in print. The calendar, CSP Foreign vii no. 714 §2 (Avignon, 1 Oct 1564, P.S. 4 Oct, "portions in cipher, deciphered"), paraphrases the same passages in the same order and is used only to set them (decipher_C.md gives it row by row). Each paragraph below is a run of consecutive passages; ";" separates passages that do not join. Marks: [?word] = carried only in part by its signs (the dot placements on the hooked-T code signs are not stable in Forbes's copy); [word] = supplied, not carried by the signs; […] = signs not read. The decipherment's spelling is kept. -->

# No. 10, Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Avignon, 4 October 1564

## Reading

Their designs here are two [?things]: th'one of the papists; all; papistry; the other […]; gospel; [?impossible]; all
again to the olde state; attempt[e]; the ruyne; [?heads]; the Italian and papistes.

England and Scotland, and the amities of those two [?realms]; [?and] their purposes; break the amity; if it might be,
to [?full] subjection in papistry.

Earl Murray; they; [?Lords]; be of the countreband; interrupt [?this] their; [?has] eye to these things; it […]
[?occurrents]; by litle and litle and by […] […] [?great]; [?this] [?man's] discourse.

The Bishop of Glasgow is here greater; no embassador; [?now] a more papist; his profesion and [?living] is
[?thereby]; he has an abbay in Poictiers; [great] rent to him, and his bushoprick in Scotland wholie reserved to him;
he dispacheth, and […] to [?Rome]; what practise they have; from […]; if Earl Murray […] and they of the
Congregation there; and good espials here; to his doings; he [?is] [?wise], and they be too negligent.

The marriage of the Queen of Scots trouble[th] them; their [?sure]st anker; Earl [of] Warwic[k]; […] Lord Robert;
of; prince of Spain; to marry [?the] queen; but their; two marriages for her; with [the] [Earl] [of] [Arran] with
compact to chaunge his religion and to […] [him]self [upon] Earl Murray; others of the religion as auctors of his
emprisonment.

The Queen Mother; [the] [young] Duke of Guise; two cardinals and all their [kin]; the Queen Mother; that marriage;
[that] house of Guise stronger, which is too stronge; in France to be; [John] Babtista; [?whom] they; into Scotland;
he is sick; in London; they; [?either]; his dispache; they are [?in]; there; be a privy practise [betwixt] Earl Lenox
and her, to marry his sonne. [?Queen] [?of] [?Scots] […]; either [the] [said] to the young Duke or to the Earl; they
feare that [?she] [is] enclined to this last offer of Lenox.

No. 11, Smith to Cecil, Marseilles, 13 Nov 1564 (R9253, f. 166)

Smith to Cecil, Marseilles, 13 Nov 1564: never in so great a trouble; a post for Scotland goes by Dieppe or by sea, all by the Cardinal of Lorraine; the matter is hot, chiefly the marriage matter, who shall bear the bell away; his men in England by whom he dare speak (CSP vii 793).

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

DECODE R9252 p.2 (IMG_R9252_I43269_P2), Add MS 4136 f.164. CSP Foreign vol. 7 no. 642 ("portions in cipher,
deciphered"), §3.

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.
Decryption

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

| 1 | ẗ ḣ ⊔ξ⊓⊢̈ ϖ6:⊢̈L / ·1 p· :b m̲ ⊔ꝏπ2X2:6310· ℓ̲ ξωπγω ẏ :s M ξ⊔10· ẅ / :ʒba ẅ ḣ ξL8·56·6:Lξ 1 ṙ n̲ f: | this [bearer] Adam Hume, I p[erceive] by Mr Alingtons letter which you sent me, was well rewarded. What he deserved I can not tell | ✓ |

T Enos ·o ⩓10· / ḣ ξ⊔8:L A: ẗ 2:9:⊢̈L | it was he, at the [least] he was one of them, which with lies and forged invencions to let the peace at the making thereof made Sir Nicholas almost or altogether mad. [He] lay[?] all this tyme [with] the Ambassador of Scotland | ✓ (order: the "to let the peace" line stands before :W on the page) |
| 2 cont. | ḣ ꟿ̲ a· 1 T̈ 8·6:9·8Lγ2:bo ḣ T̈ b: :s F: T 85·62:L⊔‡⊔ / 23:L10 ·o ⩓ T Enos J̣ ω6ꝏꝏL f T· 8⊔9π{{L10· ·A / a·: a· ḣ γ66:{ξ ·A Ẅ⊔ T J̣ X{⊔ξ T̈ b: 6·πξ ·o ḣ | who[?] … suspecteth him to be sent from the Protestants of Scotland. | 2 cont. | 1 ṙ 6:4ξL6·{{⊔3ξ n̅ ẗ :b ϖ6:⊢̈L 8·⊢̈⊔{{ L6·7·⊓4ξ / ẅ :a 1 ⊢̈⊔6·6:L{ | I [?] understand[?] now this by Hume['s] small errand … at I marvel[?] | not in CSP (tentative) |

(1) and (2) as far as "rid of him" are read and agree with CSP no. 806 almost word for word. The tail of (2) and (3) are

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.
Reading

no11_reading.md

<!-- Reading of no. 11, built 22 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (batch b03). Forbes copied only the 12 cipher stretches (Add MS 4136 f. 166, DECODE R9253); the clear text is not in print. Re-read on the image with Smith's key R9261 (no01-no23_reread_log.md). Setting (CSP Foreign vii no. 793): "Still calls upon him for Scotland. In so great a calm they were never here in so great a trouble ... This day there departs a post for Scotland, who goes by Dieppe or otherwise by sea; all go and come by the Cardinal of Lorraine ... The matter is hot, and is chiefly the marriage matter, and a great stir who shall bear the belle away ... still Cecil keeps his men in England, by whom he dare speak." The sign after "by" (written ·∂, the key's "doubt") stands where the calendar has "by sea" here and in no. 15. The place sign ⊙ is Calais in the key, Dieppe in the calendar. [word] = supplied, [?word] = partly carried, […] = unread. Spelling (v6, 23 Oct 2026): the figures', as Smith's key spells them (no01-no23_reread_log.md, decipher_*.md, no01-no23_corrections.tsv); the modernised spelling of 22 Oct restored by hand where the figures spell a word (code signs for names, places and titles carry no spelling and keep the key's gloss). No word was changed. Modern spelling is only in the English file. -->

# No. 11, Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Marseilles, 13 November 1564

Scotland. In as great a trouble. Marriages. In England and Scotland.

Scotland. I must […] knowledge of it; [?he] goeth by [?Dieppe]; by [?sea]; by the Cardinal of Lorraine.

The matter is hote; it is chief the marriage matter; who shall [bear] the bel awaye.

By whom I dare speak.
Translation

no11_en.md

<!-- English of no11_reading.md; setting from CSP Foreign vii no. 793. -->

# No. 11, Smith to Cecil, Marseilles, 13 November 1564 — English

Scotland. In as great a trouble (as ever). Marriages. In England and Scotland.

Scotland. I must […] knowledge of it; [?he] goes by [?Dieppe], by [?sea], by the Cardinal of Lorraine (all the Scottish
posts pass through the Cardinal).

The matter is hot; it is chiefly the marriage question (of the Queen of Scots), and who shall [carry] the prize.

By whom (Smith's own men, kept in England) I dare speak.

No. 12, Smith to Cecil, Arles, 20 Nov 1564 (R9253, f. 166) read in part

Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Arles, 20 Nov 1564: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (large part; checked against CSP F. 7 no. 806). A marginal gloss spells out "Enos" = Ambassador. In decipher_D.md, Letter D4: glyphs and plaintext per passage.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (83% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

Transcription

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

## Letter D6: Smith to Cecil, Arles 24 Nov 1564

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.
Decryption

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.

<!-- Reading of no. 12, built 23 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (verification 3) from decipher_D.md D4: the cipher passages of Add MS 4136 f. 166 (DECODE R9253), read with Smith's key R9261. Forbes copied only the cipher; the clear text is not in print. CSP Foreign vii no. 806 paraphrases passages (1) and (2) as far as "rid of him" almost word for word; the rest of (2) and passage (3) are not in the calendar. Marks: [?word] = carried only in part; [word] = supplied, not carried by the signs; p[erceive] = an abbreviated word expanded; […] = signs not read. The decipherment's spelling is kept. -->

# No. 12, Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Arles, 20 November 1564

## Reading

This [bearer] Adam Hume, I p[erceive] by Mr Alingtons letter which you sent me, was well rewarded. What he deserved I
can not tell; it was he, at the [?least] he was one of them, which with lies and forged invencions to let the peace at
the making thereof made Sir Nicholas almost or altogether mad. [He] [?lay] all this tyme [with] the Ambassador of
Scotland, [?who] […] suspecteth him to be sent from the Protestants of Scotland. The Ambassador is wholly for the
Papistes, and […] glad to be rid of him.

I […] [?understand] now this by Hume['s] small errand […] at I [?marvel]. [?Probably] he had […] [?warned] […]
[?procured] […] of him.

No. 13, Smith to Cecil, Arles, 22 Nov 1564 (R9253, f. 166)

Smith to Cecil, Arles, 22 Nov 1564: the Emperor's house (the Archduke Charles) and a marriage with the Queen's Majesty (CSP vii 808).

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.
Reading

<!-- Reading of no. 13, built 22 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (batch b03). Forbes copied only the two cipher stretches (Add MS 4136 f. 166, DECODE R9253), re-read on the image with Smith's key R9261. The first is the key's sign for the Emperor; the second is m̄ (marriage, as in nos. 11 and 15) + Ẅ (with) + T: (the key's "there") + the Queen's Majesty sign. Setting (CSP Foreign vii no. 808): "If Cecil means the Lord Darnley, men have the like opinion here. Of that matter he wrote by Beton, and of the Archduke Charles by Mathir." [?word] = partly carried. -->

The Emperor. Marriage with [?there] the Queen's Majesty.

# No. 13, Smith to Cecil, Arles, 22 November 1564 — English

The Emperor (that is, the Emperor's house: the Archduke Charles). Marriage with [?there] the Queen (Elizabeth).

No. 14, Smith to Cecil, Arles, 24 Nov 1564 (R9253, f. 166) read in part

Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Arles, 24 Nov 1564: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (all but a few signs; checked against CSP F. 7 no. 815). The Cardinal of Lorraine and the Prince of Condé banqueting together at Soissons and Nanteuil, the Guise complaint that the Cardinal's letters are opened at Paris and Lyons, and the bearer Hume "altogether a Lidington ... having pencions of all three princes". In decipher_D.md, Letter D6: glyphs and plaintext per passage.

Builds coherent sentences, but under the read bar of 95% of words read as sense (93% here); the holes are marked in the reading.

Transcription

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.
Decryption

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.
Reading

# No. 14, Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Arles, 24 November 1564

## Reading

The Cardinal of Lorraine and the Prince of Condé have had conferens and familiar banketting togither at Soissons and
at Nanteuil le chasteau, which was the late [?Cardinal] of Burbons, and all to […] […]. This is much noted here.

They of Guise do complaine that all letters that come from the Cardinal, and the […], be [?opened] and […],
principally at Paris and Lyons. This at this […]; and the Embassatour of Scotland and the Cardinal and Madame de Guise
be daily in great dispeches to and from Scotland and to and from the Cardinal of Lorraine, and all by […].

This bearer Hume is altogether a Lidington, whom you shall find double, I am afraid, or rather triple, having […] and
pencions of all three princes.

No. 15, Smith to Cecil, Narbonne, 7 Jan 1564/5 (R9254, f. 167) decoded, not read

Smith to Cecil, Narbonne, 7 Jan 1564/5: posts out of Scotland, Mr Beton and Hume by land, by the Cardinal of Lorraine, by sea; the house of Guise earnest in the marriage between the Duke of Guise and the Queen of Scots (CSP vii 916).

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

Transcription

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

All read, and 1 only in part. Here ":F" means French, not "favour" as in the key.

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.
Decryption

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

## Letter D1: Smith to Cecil, Valence 1 Sep 1564
DECODE R9252 p.2 (IMG_R9252_I43269_P2), Add MS 4136 f.164. CSP Foreign vol. 7 no. 642 ("portions in cipher,

| # | glyphs (normalized) | plaintext | CSP 642 agreement |

| 2 | ḧ-like sign | ⟨code⟩ | — |
| 3 | ⩓ | Scotland | ✓ |

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.
Decryption

decipher_D.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136: decipherment D (letters of Sept 1564 to Feb 1565)

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

Readings come from the glyphs through KEY.md and the code words confirmed in decipher_A.md. CSP Foreign vol. 7 was
used afterwards to confirm them (csp/v7_pp195-215.html, v7_pp234-252.html, v7_pp277-290.html, v7_pp290-306.html).
Glyphs are normalised as in decipher_A. `[?]` marks an uncertain letter or word and `⟨..⟩` an unread code sign.
Clear-English nulls (Andwerpe, garnesey, don, and, yf, sandwich) are left out of the plaintext.

New points about the system, taken from these letters:
- **Enos = Ambassador.** In the 20 Nov letter it is a code word ("T Enos .o ⩓" = the Ambassador of Scotland), and a
  marginal gloss there spells it out (`Enos = ⊓⊢̈‡⊔ξ⊔2:66·`). It is not a null here. The key's top line agrees
  ("enos Ambassador").
- **m̄ / M̈ = marriage / marry.** Read from context in 1 Sep (25), Narbonne 7 Jan and 22 Nov (2). This is not in the key
  as read.
- **The flag sign (⊐ with a stroke)** is used twice, and both times the context is Archduke Charles: 22 Nov (1) and
  Toulouse (6). It is probably the key's "the Emperor" sign, used for the Emperor's house.
- **B: = bishop** and **:L = Lord**, as in the key. A kite-shaped ξ with a hook is w (were, which, was, lies…), while
  the plain ξ is d (dare, died).
- **ω10· / ⌐ω** after "the late": this could be the Cardinal sign in a variant form ⟨?⟩.

## Letter D4: Smith to Cecil, Arles 20 Nov 1564

## Summary
- **1 Sep 1564 (CSP 642):** 28 of 31 passages read. They give the whole cipher of §3: the Scots–French practice, the
  Lord of Murray, Huntly, the bishops and the Pope's counsel, Lethington, the Bishop of Glasgow and the pension, Lord
  Robert, the old Pope, and the King of Spain and the Queen of Scots.
- **3 Sep (CSP 650):** 2 of 2 read (Nanion Cocborne, Villeros).
- **Marseilles 13 Nov (CSP 793):** read in part (Scotland, great trouble, by the Cardinal, "the matter is hote", the
  marriage matter, "by whom I dare speak").
- **Arles 20 Nov (CSP 806):** the Adam Hume passage is read in large part, as far as "glad to be rid of him". The last
  lines are tentative and not in CSP.
- **Arles 22 Nov (CSP 808):** 2 short passages, read only in part (the Archduke Charles sign; marriage with the
  Queen's Majesty [?]).
- **Arles 24 Nov (CSP 815):** the whole 12-line block is read except a few code signs.
- **Narbonne 7 Jan 1565 (CSP 916):** read in large part.
- **Toulouse 9 Feb 1565 (CSP 979):** 10 of 10 read, and 1 only in part.
- Every reading was made from the glyphs and then checked against CSP. No passage contradicts the calendar, and no
  crib was forced. The unread items are code signs that are not in the key as read: ⟨J̣⟩, ⟨ẓ⟩, ⟨ï⟩, ⟨C·⟩, ⟨F̄⟩, the
  (2) sign of 1 Sep, and Marseilles (3) and (10).
- Key additions: Enos = Ambassador (a code word, not a null); m̄/M̈ = marriage/marry; :F = French; the flag sign is
  probably the Emperor's house / Archduke Charles; the kite ξ = w and the plain ξ = d.
Reading

no16_reading.md

<!-- Reading of no. 16, built 22 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (batch b03). Forbes copied only the 10 cipher stretches (Add MS 4136 f. 167, DECODE R9254 p. 1), re-read on the image with Smith's key R9261. Passage 2 (congratula-) and the second line of passage 1 (-cion and condoleaunce) are one phrase split by the copy. The flag sign is the key's "the Emperor"; :F = French; ö = one (the calendar has "this French offer"). Setting (CSP Foreign vii no. 979): "so good occasions being offered by congratulation and condolence, the time has been so lost ... What this tickling of the French and this desire of interview means, he cannot tell. In his letters to the Queen ... If they have any understanding of Charles of Austria ... The French have time enough to dally; the Queen not so. Knows not how the Queen does or will or may take this French offer." […] = unread signs (n̲ T̈ m̤ ! T. ɛ̈). Spelling (v6, 23 Oct 2026): the figures', as Smith's key spells them (no01-no23_reread_log.md, decipher_*.md, no01-no23_corrections.tsv); the modernised spelling of 22 Oct restored by hand where the figures spell a word (code signs for names, places and titles carry no spelling and keep the key's gloss). No word was changed. Modern spelling is only in the English file. -->

# No. 16, Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Toulouse, 9 February 1564/5

[…] in the […] congratulacion and condoleaunce.

Of the French; of intervewe; the Queen's Majesty; the Emperor; [the] French; the Queen's Majesty; the Queen's Majesty;
French one.

no16_en.md

<!-- English of no16_reading.md; setting from CSP Foreign vii no. 979. -->

# No. 16, Smith to Cecil, Toulouse, 9 February 1565 — English

[…] in the […] congratulation and condolence (the occasions offered and lost).

Of the French; of (the proposed) interview; the Queen; the Emperor (his house: Charles of Austria); [the] French; the
Queen; the Queen; (this) French one (offer).

No. 17, Smith to Cecil, 22 Mar 1565/6 (R9250, ff. 159-60)

Smith to Cecil, Bourbon-l'Archambault, 22 Mar 1565/6: not that crown; the young King and Queen of Scots; intelligence and practices in England and foreign realms; the Pope's and King of Spain's hands; privy dispatches and couriers out of Scotland; the ambassadors of Spain and Scotland and the Cardinal of Lorraine; the Bishop of Glasgow looks to be a cardinal; popery in Scotland and England; a fat Scottish woman, married, the conveyer of letters to Lady Lennox (CSP viii 208).

decipher_A.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136 — decipherment A

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

Key: KEY.md / decode/IMG_R9261_I43290_P1.jpg. Glyph transcription is normalized: digits with `.`/`:` as written,
`⊢̈` = m, `{`/`}` = l, `ξ` = d, `L` = e, `⊔ ⊓` = a, `γ ∝ α` = c, `π` = i, `X` = g, `ω` = h, `□` = f, `‡` = b,
`◇—◇` = w; code words given as `sign(word)`. Clear-English nulls omitted. [?] = uncertain letter/word,
⟨code⟩ = unread sign.

## Letter 4 — Smith to Cecil, Paris, 25 Jan 1563/4
DECODE R9250 P1 (long block), Add MS 4136 f.159. CSP Foreign vol. 7 no. 103 ("Orig., portions in cipher
deciphered"). Passages (1)-(2) only numbered; the block is one long stretch. Read as runs; not every group
resolved.

- (1) `464: ö{10: T. :F` — "not only the French[?]" [?]
- (2) `T ⊓⊢̈‡⊔⊓9.10.⊔ξ66. .o ⊡ ṡ 6o[?] ⊢̈L ξ67.` — "the ambassador of Spain send[s] me word"

## Summary
- 27 Jan 1562/3: 32/32 read, word-for-word agreement with Forbes II 315-318.
- 13 Apr 1563: 12/12 read, agrees with Forbes II 384-386 (passage 7 first word uncertain).
- 27 July 1563 (to the Queen): 2 passages, read in part ("my Lord of Warwick … be able to keep … men he hath");
  fits CSP vi no. 1078 (dated 28 July).
- 25 Jan 1563/4: long block read in large part; agrees with CSP vii no. 103; last line tentative.
- 22 Mar 1566/7 (= 22 Mar 1566, CSP viii no. 208): 27 passages, about 12 firm, the rest uncertain (short code
  groups like 1, 2, 4, 10, 11, 15, 20, 25-26 unread).
- 29 Apr 1566: 5/5 read, agrees with CSP viii no. 327.
- The key works as summarized in KEY.md; scribal slips seen: 5. for 5: (v), 4 for L (e), ⊔ for π in a few words.
- The reading was done from the glyphs; the cribs confirmed it but I did not write in crib words that the glyphs
  do not support. Where a word appears only because of the crib it is marked [?].
Decryption

decipher_A.md

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136 — decipherment A

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

Key: KEY.md / decode/IMG_R9261_I43290_P1.jpg. Glyph transcription is normalized: digits with `.`/`:` as written,
`⊢̈` = m, `{`/`}` = l, `ξ` = d, `L` = e, `⊔ ⊓` = a, `γ ∝ α` = c, `π` = i, `X` = g, `ω` = h, `□` = f, `‡` = b,
`◇—◇` = w; code words given as `sign(word)`. Clear-English nulls omitted. [?] = uncertain letter/word,
⟨code⟩ = unread sign.

## Summary
- 27 Jan 1562/3: 32/32 read, word-for-word agreement with Forbes II 315-318.
- 13 Apr 1563: 12/12 read, agrees with Forbes II 384-386 (passage 7 first word uncertain).
- 27 July 1563 (to the Queen): 2 passages, read in part ("my Lord of Warwick … be able to keep … men he hath");
  fits CSP vi no. 1078 (dated 28 July).
- 25 Jan 1563/4: long block read in large part; agrees with CSP vii no. 103; last line tentative.
- 22 Mar 1566/7 (= 22 Mar 1566, CSP viii no. 208): 27 passages, about 12 firm, the rest uncertain (short code
  groups like 1, 2, 4, 10, 11, 15, 20, 25-26 unread).
- 29 Apr 1566: 5/5 read, agrees with CSP viii no. 327.
- The key works as summarized in KEY.md; scribal slips seen: 5. for 5: (v), 4 for L (e), ⊔ for π in a few words.
- The reading was done from the glyphs; the cribs confirmed it but I did not write in crib words that the glyphs
  do not support. Where a word appears only because of the crib it is marked [?].
