no17_reading.md

<!-- Reading of no. 17, built 22 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (batch b03). Forbes copied only the 27 cipher stretches (Add MS 4136 ff. 159-160, DECODE R9250 pp. 1-2; his heading "22 Mar 1566/7" is 22 March 1565/6), re-read on the images with Smith's key R9261 (decipher_A.md Letter 5 corrected; no01-no23_reread_log.md). The clear text is not in print. Setting (CSP Foreign viii no. 208): "it is not the Scottish crown which that young King and Queen look for, but a bigger one, and that they have more intelligence and practices in England and in other realms than Cecil thinks for ... Both the Pope and the King of Spain's hands are deeper in that dish than he knows. David Anstruther went ... through England into Scotland, and at the same time Thornton by sea. The Ambassadors of Spain and Scotland and the Cardinal of Lorraine be too great in their devices ... The Bishop of Glasgow looks to be cardinal, and to bring popery ... not only in Scotland but also in England. A Scottish woman, dwelling in Westminster, who was married to a captain, is the conveyor of letters which the messengers bring out of Scotland to Lady Lennox." One line per run of passages. [?word] = partly carried, […] = unread sign. Spelling (v6, 23 Oct 2026): the figures', as Smith's key spells them (no01-no23_reread_log.md, decipher_*.md, no01-no23_corrections.tsv); the modernised spelling of 22 Oct restored by hand where the figures spell a word (code signs for names, places and titles carry no spelling and keep the key's gloss). No word was changed. Modern spelling is only in the English file. -->

# No. 17, Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Bourbon-l'Archambault, 22 March 1565/6

[?Not] that crown. Young [?King] and Queen. Intelligence and practices in England. Forain realmes.

The Pope and the King of Spain['s] [?hands] be in that. French; French; that.

That had both one dispach. Have seen. Carried and one […] brought the. A […] privy dispach of. Nor. From Scotland.
[?Go] unserched.

The Ambassadors of Spain and Scotland and the Cardinal of Lorraine. Overmuch; Bishop of Glasgow lokes to be a
cardinal; popery; Scotland but also England.

Which long [?came] out of Scotland. A fat Scotish woman; she was married; she is the conveyer of; out of Scotland to the
[…] Lennox. From her; the Queen's […]; her to; be discovered and […] practices.

no17_en.md

<!-- English of no17_reading.md; setting from CSP Foreign viii no. 208. -->

# No. 17, Smith to Cecil, Bourbon-l'Archambault, 22 March 1566 — English

[?Not] that crown (the Scottish one; they look for a bigger). (That) young [?King] and Queen (Darnley and Mary). Intelligence
and practices in England. Foreign realms.

The Pope's and the King of Spain's [?hands] are in that (dish). French; French; that.

That had both one dispatch. Have seen. Carried, and one […] brought the. A […] secret dispatch of. Nor. From Scotland.
[?Go] unsearched.

The Ambassadors of Spain and Scotland and the Cardinal of Lorraine (consult together) overmuch; the Bishop of Glasgow
expects to be made a cardinal; popery; (not only in) Scotland but also (in) England.

Which (letters) long [?came] out of Scotland. A fat Scottish woman; she was married (to a captain); she is the carrier of
(letters) out of Scotland to the […] (Lady) Lennox. From her; the Queen's […]; her to; be discovered, and […] practices.

No. 18, Smith to Cecil, Paris, 29 Apr 1566 (R9250, f. 160)

Smith to Cecil, Paris, 29 Apr 1566: Sturm's letter by Peter Clare, Almain; Albert de Rosemberg; a continuation with the princes of Almain for the recovery of Metz and Calais (CSP viii 327).

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136 — decipherment A

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

Key: KEY.md / decode/IMG_R9261_I43290_P1.jpg. Glyph transcription is normalized: digits with `.`/`:` as written,
`⊢̈` = m, `{`/`}` = l, `ξ` = d, `L` = e, `⊔ ⊓` = a, `γ ∝ α` = c, `π` = i, `X` = g, `ω` = h, `□` = f, `‡` = b,
`◇—◇` = w; code words given as `sign(word)`. Clear-English nulls omitted. [?] = uncertain letter/word,
⟨code⟩ = unread sign.

## Summary
- 27 Jan 1562/3: 32/32 read, word-for-word agreement with Forbes II 315-318.
- 13 Apr 1563: 12/12 read, agrees with Forbes II 384-386 (passage 7 first word uncertain).
- 27 July 1563 (to the Queen): 2 passages, read in part ("my Lord of Warwick … be able to keep … men he hath");
  fits CSP vi no. 1078 (dated 28 July).
- 25 Jan 1563/4: long block read in large part; agrees with CSP vii no. 103; last line tentative.
- 22 Mar 1566/7 (= 22 Mar 1566, CSP viii no. 208): 27 passages, about 12 firm, the rest uncertain (short code
  groups like 1, 2, 4, 10, 11, 15, 20, 25-26 unread).
- 29 Apr 1566: 5/5 read, agrees with CSP viii no. 327.
- The key works as summarized in KEY.md; scribal slips seen: 5. for 5: (v), 4 for L (e), ⊔ for π in a few words.
- The reading was done from the glyphs; the cribs confirmed it but I did not write in crib words that the glyphs
  do not support. Where a word appears only because of the crib it is marked [?].

# Smith ciphers in BL Add MS 4136 — decipherment A

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

Key: KEY.md / decode/IMG_R9261_I43290_P1.jpg. Glyph transcription is normalized: digits with `.`/`:` as written,
`⊢̈` = m, `{`/`}` = l, `ξ` = d, `L` = e, `⊔ ⊓` = a, `γ ∝ α` = c, `π` = i, `X` = g, `ω` = h, `□` = f, `‡` = b,
`◇—◇` = w; code words given as `sign(word)`. Clear-English nulls omitted. [?] = uncertain letter/word,
⟨code⟩ = unread sign.

## Letter 1 — Smith to Cecil, Paris, 27 Jan 1562/3
DECODE R9249 (IMG_R9249_I43259_P), Add MS 4136 f.158. Printed: Forbes, *A Full View*, vol. II pp. 315-318
(forbes2.txt lines ~26540-26832). Every passage below aligns with the printed text.

Letter 1: 32/32 passages read; the text agrees with Forbes throughout. Small scribal slips (5. for v, stray
signs) marked [?].

## Letter 2 — Smith to Cecil, 13 Apr 1563
DECODE R9250 (IMG_R9250_I43261_P1, top), Add MS 4136 f.159. Printed: Forbes vol. II pp. 384-386 (forbes2.txt
lines ~31780-32010; the letter is dated at the foot "the xiii of Aprill 1563"). Aligned with the printed text.

All 12 read; agrees with Forbes.

## Letter 3 — Smith to the Queen, 27 July 1563
DECODE R9250 P1, Add MS 4136 f.159. Not in Forbes as such; CSP Foreign vol. 6 no. 1078 (Smith to the Queen,
Valmont 28 July 1563, §5: the Earl of Warwick's letter "does not answer whether he be able to keep it, or for what
time, nor what men he has in it"). Forbes's date 27 July may be the date of drafting; the content fits no. 1078.

| # | glyphs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ⊢̈9:(my) ⊽76.ξ .o ◇—◇⊓6.◇πγθ | my Lord of Warwick |
| 2 | ·◇[?] ġ[?] b: ⊓‡⊽L T̈ θL9 ⊓2:76.4 6yf 377:f[?] / Ẍ[?] 4.L6.⊢̈L[?] ·A 465. ö ⊢̈L4 ḥ ω⊓3: ϖπ4 ⊔4: | … be able to keep [the] town … [for how long] … and how many men he hath in it [?] — only "be able to keep" and "men he hath" are firm; rest ⟨uncertain⟩ |

Read in part; the firm words match CSP's summary.

## Letter 4 — Smith to Cecil, Paris, 25 Jan 1563/4
DECODE R9250 P1 (long block), Add MS 4136 f.159. CSP Foreign vol. 7 no. 103 ("Orig., portions in cipher
deciphered"). Passages (1)-(2) only numbered; the block is one long stretch. Read as runs; not every group
resolved.

- (1) `464: ö{10: T. :F` — "not only the French[?]" [?]
- (2) `T ⊓⊢̈‡⊔⊓9.10.⊔ξ66. .o ⊡ ṡ 6o[?] ⊢̈L ξ67.` — "the ambassador of Spain send[s] me word"
  `:T ḥ ω⊽[?] ḥ G.` "that he hath good" `⊔ξ5:L7.3:π9.L⊢̈L24:` "advertisement" `:T T :F[?] ṁ: ⟨?⟩ ⊓{ξL7.10. ·A ∞. 9.6:γω L4.5:⊔9…` "that the French make ladders and other such equipages"
  `⊓2: L9.4:⊔8⊽L10. ·A ⊚` "at Estaples and Boulogne" `ẁ ζ̣ ⊢̈L⊓4 T̈ 9.5:986.L⊓+4 ö[?] … 2⊓4 … 凸` "whether they mean to surprise [some] town [in] England"
  … `T. ⊽6◇ γ7 5?24:6.L10:` "[or attempt some place in] the Low Countries"
  … `.A ʃ̄ ξ 9.⊓⊔4: :T ⊡` "and he saith that the King of Spain" `L4 4:6.⊔ξ⊔3 T̈ 37 9.7:γω {Lα X5:4[?] α. +○ ϑ. ‡6.5:4:4` "[hath] entered into no such league as France doth bruit"
  `Ẅ ◩ 76. ∞. 96.⊔γL10 467. n̅ ℭ` "with the Pope or other princes, nor never will"
  `.A :T ḥ ⊢̈⊔2ξL2:ω ʃ. ⊓X⊓π4 9.4: T. 4.5:L34 .o 4⊓6:⊓6.7.4` "and that he mindeth nothing against the queen of Navarre"
  `‡5:4: T̈ ⊽⊔5:4⊔4 2.5:πL4:[?]` "but to live [in quiet]" `·A T̈ ⊢̈⊓θL ω⊔⊢̈ 9.L⊽□ 64 R:` "and to make himself rich"
  `.A 5.Lγ75.L7. :T 2:6.L⊓9.5:6.4: ẃ ξ □⊓2:ω L7. ḥ 9.9L44: Ẅ75:4: …` "and recover that treasure which his father had s[pent] without …" ⟨rest of last line uncertain⟩

Read in large part; everything read agrees with CSP no. 103 (ladders at Étaples/Boulogne, surprise of a town in
England or the Low Countries, no league with the Pope, nothing against the Queen of Navarre, "make himself rich").
The final clause about the father's treasure is not in CSP's summary and is tentative.

(Bourbon l'Archambault, 22 March 1566, "partly in cipher"): Forbes's "1566/7" heading looks wrong by a year —
there is no Smith letter of 22 March 1567 in CSP vol. 8 and Smith was then in England.

All 5 read (tail of 3 uncertain); agrees with CSP no. 327.

## Summary
- 27 Jan 1562/3: 32/32 read, word-for-word agreement with Forbes II 315-318.
- 13 Apr 1563: 12/12 read, agrees with Forbes II 384-386 (passage 7 first word uncertain).
- 27 July 1563 (to the Queen): 2 passages, read in part ("my Lord of Warwick … be able to keep … men he hath");
  fits CSP vi no. 1078 (dated 28 July).
- 25 Jan 1563/4: long block read in large part; agrees with CSP vii no. 103; last line tentative.
- 22 Mar 1566/7 (= 22 Mar 1566, CSP viii no. 208): 27 passages, about 12 firm, the rest uncertain (short code
  groups like 1, 2, 4, 10, 11, 15, 20, 25-26 unread).
- 29 Apr 1566: 5/5 read, agrees with CSP viii no. 327.
- The key works as summarized in KEY.md; scribal slips seen: 5. for 5: (v), 4 for L (e), ⊔ for π in a few words.
- The reading was done from the glyphs; the cribs confirmed it but I did not write in crib words that the glyphs
  do not support. Where a word appears only because of the crib it is marked [?].
Reading

# No. 18, Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Paris, 29 April 1566

This letter. Peter Clare. Almain, and also […]. Albert de Rosemberg.

Continuation with the princes of Almain for the recovery of Metz and Calais.

<!-- English of no18_reading.md; setting from CSP Foreign viii no. 327. -->

# No. 18, Smith to Cecil, Paris, 29 April 1566 — English

This letter (from Johann Sturm). Peter Clare. German, and also […]. Albert von Rosenberg.

A continued alliance with the German princes for the recovery of Metz and Calais.

No. 19, Smith to Cecil, 15 Oct 1562 (R9236, f. 140)

Smith to Cecil, Paris, 15 Oct 1562 (Forbes ii 119): Scots report envoys sent to move the Queen of Scots to break with England and make war, and a plan to convey the hostages into Flanders; Secretary Rollet and Monsieur Villemort are gone.

Transcription

decipher_E.md

# R9236 (Add MS 4136 f.140): five letters, Oct 1562 - Nov 1563, read with key R9261

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

# Smith to Cecil, Add MS 4136 f.140 (DECODE R9236), early blocks

Image decode/IMG_R9236_I43221_P.jpg. Key: ./KEY.md. Glyph conventions as in ./decipher_A.md.
Blocks 1 and 3 are printed in Forbes, *A Full View* vol. II (./forbes2.txt): 15 Oct 1562 at p.119
(lines ~10613-10622), 15/17 Dec 1562 at pp.237-238 (lines ~20633-20645). The glyph readings agree with
Forbes. I did not find block 2 (20 Oct 1562) in Forbes; it is read from the glyphs only.
[?] = uncertain.

## 1. 15 Oct 1562 (y ~380-770)
Forbes: "I am certaynlie advertised by diverse Scots, that there be divers sent allredie ... to move the queen of
Scots to break with you, and to make war out of hand; and that the mater is now in hand ... that the hostages
which you have shall be convoyed of into Flanders ... secretary Rollet and monsieur Vyllemort."

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | arrow-triangle | Scots |
| 2 | ▷— | Queen of Scots |
| 3 | ‡ 5 L ⊔ θ · and(null) · Ẅ ẏ ·A T̈ m: ẇ6[?] | break with you and to make war (5 for 5. r; the last sign, a dotted w, looks like a war code word) |
| 4 | ·T T. ω 9 3 ⊔ X L 8 / :W ẏ ·h ·ſ 6: ς̣ξ | that the hostages [9/3 for o/s/t, dots dropped] which you have shall be [6: for b:?] convoyed (ς̣ = C. convoy + ξ d) |
| 5 | ▥ | Flanders |
| 6 | ġ ⊔ 6 9: · 5. 6 ξ} 5 3: · ⊓ 2 ξ · ⊢̈ y: / 7: 9 {ξ ω L ⊢ 7 6. 3: | secretary[?] Rollet and Monsieur Vyllemort (first word read from Forbes, glyphs do not spell it clearly; the last word spells "…l e m o r t") |

Not yet explained: line 3 "7:9{ξωL⊢76.3:" was taken as the second line of (6), Vyllemort (the ending "L⊢76.3:" = e-m-o-r-t, with ⊢ missing its dots); the opening "7:9{ξω" is not letter-perfect for "Vyll".
Forbes has "out of hand" after "war", and no glyphs stand for it.

## 2. 20 Oct 1562 (y ~1100-1400)
| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | —⊙ | Newhaven |
| 2 | X π 6 ξ L 3 / 9 π ξ L | g-i-o-d-e-n / p-i-d-e. Unresolved; perhaps "Gideon[?] side[?]" (9 for 9. s) |
| 3 | Dover(null) · α ω ⊔ 3 π ξ} ⊥⊥ 6 3 · m a ‡ γ · ⅊⅊8[?] · T̈ · ω π ξ · ⟨blot⟩ 6 3 · T̈ | Chatillon[?] (c-h-a-n[t]-i-ll-i-o-n) … "mabc" [?] … to … hid[e][?] … [?]on … to. Only "Chatillon" is fairly firm; the rest is unresolved (a blot covers one sign) |
| 4 | +○ | French / France |

## 3. 15/17 Dec 1562 (pasted slip, y ~1770-2500)
Forbes (15 Dec): "Methincks, there is no good to be done that way for Calais ... I se, you must hold ferme and
fast: Frenchmen will com to reason, but compellid. Ye must give marvelous warning to My Lorde of Warwick to
trust no Frenchman, of what religion soever he be; and that he take marvelous hid of treason, for in that is all
their trust: for whither thei fight with the prince of Conde, which I do not beleve, or they agree within
themselfes, which is most to be suspectid; as sone as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force."

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ⊙— | Calais |
| 2 | and yf(nulls) ·1 8.L ẏ ⊢̈ 5:9.2: ω7}ξ / □L5.⊢̈L ·A □⊓9. +○ M. ℭ γ7⊢̈ T̈ ξ̣ ‡5:2: γ7⊢̈9L}}πξ London(null) | I see, you must hold ferme and fast: Frenchmen will come to r[eason] (ξ̣, a single sign read as the reason code) but compellid |
| 3 | bycause(null) T̈ ⊄̣[trust] n̅ +○ M. .o ẁ 6.L}πXπ72 ◌ ē ξ b: hastings(null) | to trust no Frenchman of what religion so ever he be |
| 3a | F. Lyn(null) | treason[?] (F. taken as the treason code word, following Forbes; the key image does not confirm it) |
| 4 | f ·1 ·T ʃ A: T·7· | for in that is all their trust (the last two signs read as their + trust [?]) |
| 5 | ⊖| :W 1 ẟ n̲ ‡:? }L7:L for(null) | the Prince of Condé, which I do not believe |
| 6 | :W ʃ o: T̈ b: 10.6:9.8Lγ2:πξ / a. b[?]. a. ē T̈ b: ⊔‡}L T̈[they] γ̈ 2:ωπ3ωL7. Ẅ[will] A: T6. ⊞77.γL and(null) | which is most to be suspected; as soon as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force |

## Unresolved
- Block 1: the dotted w at the end of (3) is taken as "war"; the first word of (6) (ġ⊔69:) is read as secretary only because of Forbes.
- Block 2: passage (2), and all of (3) except Chatillon[?]. The letter was not found in Forbes.
- Block 3: "F." is read as treason and "T·7·" as their trust from Forbes; ⊞ (boxed f) = f in "force".

## Crib checks added after the first pass (21 Sept 2026)
- 20 Oct 1562 = Forbes ii 122 (CSP F. 5 no. 880, "a few words in cipher, deciphered"). Plaintext of the passage:
  "[Newhaven] is not so strong as supposed; if it were summer they would attempt to afray our men thence with a
  garden [(2) Xπ6ξL3 = garden, 9πξL = side] of chamillion [(3), first read 'Chatillon'] upon the hill on this side ...
  things begun there by [(4) France] be not perfit." All four passages now read (glyphs + Forbes).
- 4 Nov 1563 = CSP F. 6 no. 1366: "De Montaigne, whose carte of La Rochelle he sends herewith, and under another
  pretence has given him letters to the Lord Robert and Mr. Secretary, to whom he wrote from Rouen of the same matter.
  The other letters he carried himself, and in the feat whereof he wrote, thinks he can do, but this at this time is
  most necessary." The unread group "dis?che" = la Rochelle. Read in full.
- 31 Jan 1562/3 advertisements: not in Forbes, not found in CSP F. 6 (Jan 26-31, Feb 1-5). (2) = "the queen of
  Navarre" (name sign = French King / K. of Navarre, used with 'queen of'). (1) "Monsieur de [?]uy", (3) and the last
  line remain fragments. This is the only part of R9236 not read.

- Second pass on 31 Jan: last line reads '[?] band that [K. of Navarre] and [?day] with …'; context in CSP F. 6 (Warwick, late Jan 1563: 'the bruit of the Queen of Navarre's band'). Still not read.

# R9236 (Add MS 4136 f.140): five letters, Oct 1562 - Nov 1563, read with key R9261

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

# Smith to Cecil, Add MS 4136 f.140 (DECODE R9236), early blocks

Image decode/IMG_R9236_I43221_P.jpg. Key: ./KEY.md. Glyph conventions as in ./decipher_A.md.
Blocks 1 and 3 are printed in Forbes, *A Full View* vol. II (./forbes2.txt): 15 Oct 1562 at p.119
(lines ~10613-10622), 15/17 Dec 1562 at pp.237-238 (lines ~20633-20645). The glyph readings agree with
Forbes. I did not find block 2 (20 Oct 1562) in Forbes; it is read from the glyphs only.
[?] = uncertain.

## 1. 15 Oct 1562 (y ~380-770)
Forbes: "I am certaynlie advertised by diverse Scots, that there be divers sent allredie ... to move the queen of
Scots to break with you, and to make war out of hand; and that the mater is now in hand ... that the hostages
which you have shall be convoyed of into Flanders ... secretary Rollet and monsieur Vyllemort."

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | arrow-triangle | Scots |
| 2 | ▷— | Queen of Scots |
| 3 | ‡ 5 L ⊔ θ · and(null) · Ẅ ẏ ·A T̈ m: ẇ6[?] | break with you and to make war (5 for 5. r; the last sign, a dotted w, looks like a war code word) |
| 4 | ·T T. ω 9 3 ⊔ X L 8 / :W ẏ ·h ·ſ 6: ς̣ξ | that the hostages [9/3 for o/s/t, dots dropped] which you have shall be [6: for b:?] convoyed (ς̣ = C. convoy + ξ d) |
| 5 | ▥ | Flanders |
| 6 | ġ ⊔ 6 9: · 5. 6 ξ} 5 3: · ⊓ 2 ξ · ⊢̈ y: / 7: 9 {ξ ω L ⊢ 7 6. 3: | secretary[?] Rollet and Monsieur Vyllemort (first word read from Forbes, glyphs do not spell it clearly; the last word spells "…l e m o r t") |

Not yet explained: line 3 "7:9{ξωL⊢76.3:" was taken as the second line of (6), Vyllemort (the ending "L⊢76.3:" = e-m-o-r-t, with ⊢ missing its dots); the opening "7:9{ξω" is not letter-perfect for "Vyll".
Forbes has "out of hand" after "war", and no glyphs stand for it.

## 2. 20 Oct 1562 (y ~1100-1400)
| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | —⊙ | Newhaven |
| 2 | X π 6 ξ L 3 / 9 π ξ L | g-i-o-d-e-n / p-i-d-e. Unresolved; perhaps "Gideon[?] side[?]" (9 for 9. s) |
| 3 | Dover(null) · α ω ⊔ 3 π ξ} ⊥⊥ 6 3 · m a ‡ γ · ⅊⅊8[?] · T̈ · ω π ξ · ⟨blot⟩ 6 3 · T̈ | Chatillon[?] (c-h-a-n[t]-i-ll-i-o-n) … "mabc" [?] … to … hid[e][?] … [?]on … to. Only "Chatillon" is fairly firm; the rest is unresolved (a blot covers one sign) |
| 4 | +○ | French / France |

## 3. 15/17 Dec 1562 (pasted slip, y ~1770-2500)
Forbes (15 Dec): "Methincks, there is no good to be done that way for Calais ... I se, you must hold ferme and
fast: Frenchmen will com to reason, but compellid. Ye must give marvelous warning to My Lorde of Warwick to
trust no Frenchman, of what religion soever he be; and that he take marvelous hid of treason, for in that is all
their trust: for whither thei fight with the prince of Conde, which I do not beleve, or they agree within
themselfes, which is most to be suspectid; as sone as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force."

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ⊙— | Calais |
| 2 | and yf(nulls) ·1 8.L ẏ ⊢̈ 5:9.2: ω7}ξ / □L5.⊢̈L ·A □⊓9. +○ M. ℭ γ7⊢̈ T̈ ξ̣ ‡5:2: γ7⊢̈9L}}πξ London(null) | I see, you must hold ferme and fast: Frenchmen will come to r[eason] (ξ̣, a single sign read as the reason code) but compellid |
| 3 | bycause(null) T̈ ⊄̣[trust] n̅ +○ M. .o ẁ 6.L}πXπ72 ◌ ē ξ b: hastings(null) | to trust no Frenchman of what religion so ever he be |
| 3a | F. Lyn(null) | treason[?] (F. taken as the treason code word, following Forbes; the key image does not confirm it) |
| 4 | f ·1 ·T ʃ A: T·7· | for in that is all their trust (the last two signs read as their + trust [?]) |
| 5 | ⊖| :W 1 ẟ n̲ ‡:? }L7:L for(null) | the Prince of Condé, which I do not believe |
| 6 | :W ʃ o: T̈ b: 10.6:9.8Lγ2:πξ / a. b[?]. a. ē T̈ b: ⊔‡}L T̈[they] γ̈ 2:ωπ3ωL7. Ẅ[will] A: T6. ⊞77.γL and(null) | which is most to be suspected; as soon as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force |

## Unresolved
- Block 1: the dotted w at the end of (3) is taken as "war"; the first word of (6) (ġ⊔69:) is read as secretary only because of Forbes.
- Block 2: passage (2), and all of (3) except Chatillon[?]. The letter was not found in Forbes.
- Block 3: "F." is read as treason and "T·7·" as their trust from Forbes; ⊞ (boxed f) = f in "force".

no19_reading.md

<!-- Reading of no. 19, built 22 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (batch b03). Rests on Forbes, A Full View ii 119 (1741), printed from the original, with the deciphered words in italic; the 6 cipher passages of Add MS 4136 f. 140 (DECODE R9236, image fetched with the project cookie) were re-read on the image with Smith's key R9261 and agree with the italics word for word. "secretary" is a dotted sign read as the key's "secret" + ⊔69: (a-r-y, with 6 for 6. r and 9: for y); "Vyllemort" is 7:9{υL⊢76.3: (9 for 9: y) (no01-no23_reread_log.md). Cipher passages plain; ⟨ ⟩ = clear text as Forbes prints it. Spelling (v6, 23 Oct 2026): Forbes's, as printed (he prints the originals' spelling); the modernised spelling of 22 Oct was restored word by word from the OCR of Forbes vol. ii (forbes2.txt, main checkout; research/reading_audit/v6_align.py --ocr: OCR long-s read as s, an OCR form not the same word kept only when it recurs in the print, OCR slips refused by hand). Where the OCR is garbled the word keeps its earlier form; no word was changed. Modern spelling is only in the English file. -->

# No. 19, Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Paris, 15 October 1562

⟨Yow perceive, what Sir Nicholas doth thynk of ther Ambassador in England: and I am certaynlie advertised by diverse⟩
Scots, ⟨that there be divers sent allredie, and more to be sent, to move⟩ the Queen of Scots to break with you, and to
make war ⟨out of hand; and that the mater is now in hand and in traffic,⟩ that the hostages which you have shall be
convoyed ⟨of into⟩ Flanders. ⟨For the furst mater were apoyntid, and be goeng or gone,⟩ secretary Rollet and Monsieur
Vyllemort.
Translation

no19_en.md

<!-- English of no19_reading.md. ⟨ ⟩ = clear in the letter. -->

# No. 19, Smith to Cecil, Paris, 15 October 1562 — English

⟨You see what Sir Nicholas (Throckmorton) thinks of their Ambassador in England; and I am reliably told by several⟩
Scots ⟨that several men have already been sent, and more will be, to urge⟩ the Queen of Scots to break with you and to
make war ⟨at once; and that it is now in hand and being negotiated⟩ that the hostages you hold shall be conveyed ⟨away
to⟩ Flanders. ⟨For the first matter there have been appointed, and are going or gone,⟩ Secretary Rollet and Monsieur
Villemort.

No. 20, Smith to Cecil, 20 Oct 1562 (R9236, f. 140)

Smith to Cecil, Poissy, 20 Oct 1562 (Forbes ii 123): Newhaven is not so strong as men take it; in summer they would try to drive the English out with "a garden of chamillion upon the hill on this side"; the French works there are not finished.

Transcription

| 2 | and yf(nulls) ·1 8.L ẏ ⊢̈ 5:9.2: ω7}ξ / □L5.⊢̈L ·A □⊓9. +○ M. ℭ γ7⊢̈ T̈ ξ̣ ‡5:2: γ7⊢̈9L}}πξ London(null) | I see, you must hold ferme and fast: Frenchmen will come to r[eason] (ξ̣, a single sign read as the reason code) but compellid |

| 3a | F. Lyn(null) | treason[?] (F. taken as the treason code word, following Forbes; the key image does not confirm it) |
| 4 | f ·1 ·T ʃ A: T·7· | for in that is all their trust (the last two signs read as their + trust [?]) |
| 5 | ⊖| :W 1 ẟ n̲ ‡:? }L7:L for(null) | the Prince of Condé, which I do not believe |
| 6 | :W ʃ o: T̈ b: 10.6:9.8Lγ2:πξ / a. b[?]. a. ē T̈ b: ⊔‡}L T̈[they] γ̈ 2:ωπ3ωL7. Ẅ[will] A: T6. ⊞77.γL and(null) | which is most to be suspected; as soon as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force |

## Unresolved
- Block 1: the dotted w at the end of (3) is taken as "war"; the first word of (6) (ġ⊔69:) is read as secretary only because of Forbes.
- Block 2: passage (2), and all of (3) except Chatillon[?]. The letter was not found in Forbes.
- Block 3: "F." is read as treason and "T·7·" as their trust from Forbes; ⊞ (boxed f) = f in "force".
# Add MS 4136 f.140 (DECODE R9236): late blocks

New sign: `‖` = i (it fits in given, necessarie, is, in, I). The key row for `‖` is headed "ll"(?), but in this copy it reads as i.

## Crib checks added after the first pass (21 Sept 2026)
- 20 Oct 1562 = Forbes ii 122 (CSP F. 5 no. 880, "a few words in cipher, deciphered"). Plaintext of the passage:
  "[Newhaven] is not so strong as supposed; if it were summer they would attempt to afray our men thence with a
  garden [(2) Xπ6ξL3 = garden, 9πξL = side] of chamillion [(3), first read 'Chatillon'] upon the hill on this side ...
  things begun there by [(4) France] be not perfit." All four passages now read (glyphs + Forbes).
- 4 Nov 1563 = CSP F. 6 no. 1366: "De Montaigne, whose carte of La Rochelle he sends herewith, and under another
  pretence has given him letters to the Lord Robert and Mr. Secretary, to whom he wrote from Rouen of the same matter.
  The other letters he carried himself, and in the feat whereof he wrote, thinks he can do, but this at this time is
  most necessary." The unread group "dis?che" = la Rochelle. Read in full.
- 31 Jan 1562/3 advertisements: not in Forbes, not found in CSP F. 6 (Jan 26-31, Feb 1-5). (2) = "the queen of
  Navarre" (name sign = French King / K. of Navarre, used with 'queen of'). (1) "Monsieur de [?]uy", (3) and the last
  line remain fragments. This is the only part of R9236 not read.

- Second pass on 31 Jan: last line reads '[?] band that [K. of Navarre] and [?day] with …'; context in CSP F. 6 (Warwick, late Jan 1563: 'the bruit of the Queen of Navarre's band'). Still not read.

# R9236 (Add MS 4136 f.140): five letters, Oct 1562 - Nov 1563, read with key R9261

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

# Smith to Cecil, Add MS 4136 f.140 (DECODE R9236), early blocks

Image decode/IMG_R9236_I43221_P.jpg. Key: ./KEY.md. Glyph conventions as in ./decipher_A.md.
Blocks 1 and 3 are printed in Forbes, *A Full View* vol. II (./forbes2.txt): 15 Oct 1562 at p.119
(lines ~10613-10622), 15/17 Dec 1562 at pp.237-238 (lines ~20633-20645). The glyph readings agree with
Forbes. I did not find block 2 (20 Oct 1562) in Forbes; it is read from the glyphs only.
[?] = uncertain.

## 1. 15 Oct 1562 (y ~380-770)
Forbes: "I am certaynlie advertised by diverse Scots, that there be divers sent allredie ... to move the queen of
Scots to break with you, and to make war out of hand; and that the mater is now in hand ... that the hostages
which you have shall be convoyed of into Flanders ... secretary Rollet and monsieur Vyllemort."

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | arrow-triangle | Scots |
| 2 | ▷— | Queen of Scots |
| 3 | ‡ 5 L ⊔ θ · and(null) · Ẅ ẏ ·A T̈ m: ẇ6[?] | break with you and to make war (5 for 5. r; the last sign, a dotted w, looks like a war code word) |
| 4 | ·T T. ω 9 3 ⊔ X L 8 / :W ẏ ·h ·ſ 6: ς̣ξ | that the hostages [9/3 for o/s/t, dots dropped] which you have shall be [6: for b:?] convoyed (ς̣ = C. convoy + ξ d) |
| 5 | ▥ | Flanders |
| 6 | ġ ⊔ 6 9: · 5. 6 ξ} 5 3: · ⊓ 2 ξ · ⊢̈ y: / 7: 9 {ξ ω L ⊢ 7 6. 3: | secretary[?] Rollet and Monsieur Vyllemort (first word read from Forbes, glyphs do not spell it clearly; the last word spells "…l e m o r t") |

Not yet explained: line 3 "7:9{ξωL⊢76.3:" was taken as the second line of (6), Vyllemort (the ending "L⊢76.3:" = e-m-o-r-t, with ⊢ missing its dots); the opening "7:9{ξω" is not letter-perfect for "Vyll".
Forbes has "out of hand" after "war", and no glyphs stand for it.

## 2. 20 Oct 1562 (y ~1100-1400)
| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | —⊙ | Newhaven |
| 2 | X π 6 ξ L 3 / 9 π ξ L | g-i-o-d-e-n / p-i-d-e. Unresolved; perhaps "Gideon[?] side[?]" (9 for 9. s) |
| 3 | Dover(null) · α ω ⊔ 3 π ξ} ⊥⊥ 6 3 · m a ‡ γ · ⅊⅊8[?] · T̈ · ω π ξ · ⟨blot⟩ 6 3 · T̈ | Chatillon[?] (c-h-a-n[t]-i-ll-i-o-n) … "mabc" [?] … to … hid[e][?] … [?]on … to. Only "Chatillon" is fairly firm; the rest is unresolved (a blot covers one sign) |
| 4 | +○ | French / France |

## 3. 15/17 Dec 1562 (pasted slip, y ~1770-2500)
Forbes (15 Dec): "Methincks, there is no good to be done that way for Calais ... I se, you must hold ferme and
fast: Frenchmen will com to reason, but compellid. Ye must give marvelous warning to My Lorde of Warwick to
trust no Frenchman, of what religion soever he be; and that he take marvelous hid of treason, for in that is all
their trust: for whither thei fight with the prince of Conde, which I do not beleve, or they agree within
themselfes, which is most to be suspectid; as sone as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force."

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ⊙— | Calais |
| 2 | and yf(nulls) ·1 8.L ẏ ⊢̈ 5:9.2: ω7}ξ / □L5.⊢̈L ·A □⊓9. +○ M. ℭ γ7⊢̈ T̈ ξ̣ ‡5:2: γ7⊢̈9L}}πξ London(null) | I see, you must hold ferme and fast: Frenchmen will come to r[eason] (ξ̣, a single sign read as the reason code) but compellid |
| 3 | bycause(null) T̈ ⊄̣[trust] n̅ +○ M. .o ẁ 6.L}πXπ72 ◌ ē ξ b: hastings(null) | to trust no Frenchman of what religion so ever he be |
| 3a | F. Lyn(null) | treason[?] (F. taken as the treason code word, following Forbes; the key image does not confirm it) |
| 4 | f ·1 ·T ʃ A: T·7· | for in that is all their trust (the last two signs read as their + trust [?]) |
| 5 | ⊖| :W 1 ẟ n̲ ‡:? }L7:L for(null) | the Prince of Condé, which I do not believe |
| 6 | :W ʃ o: T̈ b: 10.6:9.8Lγ2:πξ / a. b[?]. a. ē T̈ b: ⊔‡}L T̈[they] γ̈ 2:ωπ3ωL7. Ẅ[will] A: T6. ⊞77.γL and(null) | which is most to be suspected; as soon as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force |

## Unresolved
- Block 1: the dotted w at the end of (3) is taken as "war"; the first word of (6) (ġ⊔69:) is read as secretary only because of Forbes.
- Block 2: passage (2), and all of (3) except Chatillon[?]. The letter was not found in Forbes.
- Block 3: "F." is read as treason and "T·7·" as their trust from Forbes; ⊞ (boxed f) = f in "force".

no20_reading.md

<!-- Reading of no. 20, built 22 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (batch b03). Rests on Forbes, A Full View ii 123 (1741), printed from the original, with the deciphered words in italic (Newhaven; garden of chamillion upon the hill on this side; France); the 4 cipher passages of Add MS 4136 f. 140 (DECODE R9236) were re-read on the image with Smith's key R9261 and agree with them. One short group (m-a-b-c, before "upon") does not decode and one sign under a blot is not legible; neither carries a word of the print (no01-no23_reread_log.md). Cipher passages plain; ⟨ ⟩ = clear text as Forbes prints it. Spelling (v6, 23 Oct 2026): Forbes's, as printed (he prints the originals' spelling); the modernised spelling of 22 Oct was restored word by word from the OCR of Forbes vol. ii (forbes2.txt, main checkout; research/reading_audit/v6_align.py --ocr: OCR long-s read as s, an OCR form not the same word kept only when it recurs in the print, OCR slips refused by hand). Where the OCR is garbled the word keeps its earlier form; no word was changed. Modern spelling is only in the English file. -->

# No. 20, Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, Poissy, 20 October 1562

Newhaven ⟨is not so strong as men do take it; and, if it were sommer, they wolde attempt to afray our men thence wythe
a⟩ garden of chamillion upon the hill on this side. ⟨They that understand the place well can understand what I meane.
And thyngs begon there by⟩ France ⟨be not perfect.⟩
Translation

no20_en.md

<!-- English of no20_reading.md. ⟨ ⟩ = clear in the letter. -->

# No. 20, Smith to Cecil, Poissy, 20 October 1562 — English

Newhaven (Le Havre) ⟨is not as strong as people think; if it were summer, they would try to drive our men out of it
with a⟩ "garden of chamillion" (Smith's veiled phrase, meaning not settled) on the hill on this side. ⟨Those who know the place will understand
what I mean. And the works begun there by⟩ the French ⟨are not finished.⟩

No. 21, Smith to Cecil, 15/17 Dec 1562 (R9236, f. 140)

Sir Thomas Smith to Cecil, 15/17 Dec 1562: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (all 6 passages; checked against Forbes ii 238). "to trust no Frenchman of what religion soever he be". In decipher_E.md, section 3: glyphs and plaintext per passage.

Transcription

| 5 | ⊖| :W 1 ẟ n̲ ‡:? }L7:L for(null) | the Prince of Condé, which I do not believe |
| 6 | :W ʃ o: T̈ b: 10.6:9.8Lγ2:πξ / a. b[?]. a. ē T̈ b: ⊔‡}L T̈[they] γ̈ 2:ωπ3ωL7. Ẅ[will] A: T6. ⊞77.γL and(null) | which is most to be suspected; as soon as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force |

## Unresolved
- Block 1: the dotted w at the end of (3) is taken as "war"; the first word of (6) (ġ⊔69:) is read as secretary only because of Forbes.
- Block 2: passage (2), and all of (3) except Chatillon[?]. The letter was not found in Forbes.
- Block 3: "F." is read as treason and "T·7·" as their trust from Forbes; ⊞ (boxed f) = f in "force".

  pretence has given him letters to the Lord Robert and Mr. Secretary, to whom he wrote from Rouen of the same matter.

  most necessary." The unread group "dis?che" = la Rochelle. Read in full.
- 31 Jan 1562/3 advertisements: not in Forbes, not found in CSP F. 6 (Jan 26-31, Feb 1-5). (2) = "the queen of
  Navarre" (name sign = French King / K. of Navarre, used with 'queen of'). (1) "Monsieur de [?]uy", (3) and the last

# Smith to Cecil, Add MS 4136 f.140 (DECODE R9236), early blocks

Image decode/IMG_R9236_I43221_P.jpg. Key: ./KEY.md. Glyph conventions as in ./decipher_A.md.
Blocks 1 and 3 are printed in Forbes, *A Full View* vol. II (./forbes2.txt): 15 Oct 1562 at p.119
(lines ~10613-10622), 15/17 Dec 1562 at pp.237-238 (lines ~20633-20645). The glyph readings agree with
Forbes. I did not find block 2 (20 Oct 1562) in Forbes; it is read from the glyphs only.
[?] = uncertain.

## 1. 15 Oct 1562 (y ~380-770)
Forbes: "I am certaynlie advertised by diverse Scots, that there be divers sent allredie ... to move the queen of
Scots to break with you, and to make war out of hand; and that the mater is now in hand ... that the hostages
which you have shall be convoyed of into Flanders ... secretary Rollet and monsieur Vyllemort."

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | arrow-triangle | Scots |
| 2 | ▷— | Queen of Scots |
| 3 | ‡ 5 L ⊔ θ · and(null) · Ẅ ẏ ·A T̈ m: ẇ6[?] | break with you and to make war (5 for 5. r; the last sign, a dotted w, looks like a war code word) |
| 4 | ·T T. ω 9 3 ⊔ X L 8 / :W ẏ ·h ·ſ 6: ς̣ξ | that the hostages [9/3 for o/s/t, dots dropped] which you have shall be [6: for b:?] convoyed (ς̣ = C. convoy + ξ d) |
| 5 | ▥ | Flanders |
| 6 | ġ ⊔ 6 9: · 5. 6 ξ} 5 3: · ⊓ 2 ξ · ⊢̈ y: / 7: 9 {ξ ω L ⊢ 7 6. 3: | secretary[?] Rollet and Monsieur Vyllemort (first word read from Forbes, glyphs do not spell it clearly; the last word spells "…l e m o r t") |

Not yet explained: line 3 "7:9{ξωL⊢76.3:" was taken as the second line of (6), Vyllemort (the ending "L⊢76.3:" = e-m-o-r-t, with ⊢ missing its dots); the opening "7:9{ξω" is not letter-perfect for "Vyll".
Forbes has "out of hand" after "war", and no glyphs stand for it.

## 2. 20 Oct 1562 (y ~1100-1400)
| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | —⊙ | Newhaven |
| 2 | X π 6 ξ L 3 / 9 π ξ L | g-i-o-d-e-n / p-i-d-e. Unresolved; perhaps "Gideon[?] side[?]" (9 for 9. s) |
| 3 | Dover(null) · α ω ⊔ 3 π ξ} ⊥⊥ 6 3 · m a ‡ γ · ⅊⅊8[?] · T̈ · ω π ξ · ⟨blot⟩ 6 3 · T̈ | Chatillon[?] (c-h-a-n[t]-i-ll-i-o-n) … "mabc" [?] … to … hid[e][?] … [?]on … to. Only "Chatillon" is fairly firm; the rest is unresolved (a blot covers one sign) |
| 4 | +○ | French / France |

## 3. 15/17 Dec 1562 (pasted slip, y ~1770-2500)
Forbes (15 Dec): "Methincks, there is no good to be done that way for Calais ... I se, you must hold ferme and
fast: Frenchmen will com to reason, but compellid. Ye must give marvelous warning to My Lorde of Warwick to
trust no Frenchman, of what religion soever he be; and that he take marvelous hid of treason, for in that is all
their trust: for whither thei fight with the prince of Conde, which I do not beleve, or they agree within
themselfes, which is most to be suspectid; as sone as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force."

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ⊙— | Calais |
| 2 | and yf(nulls) ·1 8.L ẏ ⊢̈ 5:9.2: ω7}ξ / □L5.⊢̈L ·A □⊓9. +○ M. ℭ γ7⊢̈ T̈ ξ̣ ‡5:2: γ7⊢̈9L}}πξ London(null) | I see, you must hold ferme and fast: Frenchmen will come to r[eason] (ξ̣, a single sign read as the reason code) but compellid |
| 3 | bycause(null) T̈ ⊄̣[trust] n̅ +○ M. .o ẁ 6.L}πXπ72 ◌ ē ξ b: hastings(null) | to trust no Frenchman of what religion so ever he be |
| 3a | F. Lyn(null) | treason[?] (F. taken as the treason code word, following Forbes; the key image does not confirm it) |
| 4 | f ·1 ·T ʃ A: T·7· | for in that is all their trust (the last two signs read as their + trust [?]) |
| 5 | ⊖| :W 1 ẟ n̲ ‡:? }L7:L for(null) | the Prince of Condé, which I do not believe |
| 6 | :W ʃ o: T̈ b: 10.6:9.8Lγ2:πξ / a. b[?]. a. ē T̈ b: ⊔‡}L T̈[they] γ̈ 2:ωπ3ωL7. Ẅ[will] A: T6. ⊞77.γL and(null) | which is most to be suspected; as soon as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force |

## Unresolved
- Block 1: the dotted w at the end of (3) is taken as "war"; the first word of (6) (ġ⊔69:) is read as secretary only because of Forbes.
- Block 2: passage (2), and all of (3) except Chatillon[?]. The letter was not found in Forbes.
- Block 3: "F." is read as treason and "T·7·" as their trust from Forbes; ⊞ (boxed f) = f in "force".

| 7 | ʢ6.53:L 10.7⊢̈ ʢω⊓4: ‖ 4:ωπγθ4 ξ̣[?] γ⊓4 ξ̣[?] ‡5:4: 2:ωπ9. / ⊓4: 3:ϖπ10. 2:10:⊢̈4 ‖9. ⊢̈69.4: 2LγL9.8.⊓6.‖4 | wrote somwhat, I thinke [he?] can do; but this at this tyme is most necessarie |

Running text: "...de Montaign[e] is that I [?] speake of, whose [c(h)arte?] of [dispatch?] [?] I have by here with and under
another pretence. I have given [him] letters to my Lord Robert and Mr Secretarie, and to [?] from Rouen [?] wrote of that same
matter, that [?] letters [he] carried himselfe; and in that feate [whereof] I wrote somwhat I thinke [he] can do. But this at
this tyme is most necessarie."

## Block 2: advertisements from France, 31 Jan 1562 (upright, below the signature)

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | o:(Monsieur) ξL ſ.[?]6:9: | Monsieur de [L?]uy[?] (name unread) |
| 2 | :q.(queen) .o ♁-cross[?] | the queen of [Navarre?] |
| 3 | :q. ξ̈(hath) m̲[?] L8T̈[?] 7.π5:3: 8̈[?] | the queen hath [?] ... (unread) |
| last | ξ ‡⊓2ξ T. ⊕ .a γ⊓9: Ẅ ξ ω. ξ ⊓10..H | [I?] band[?] that Rouen as [?] with ... (unread) |

## Unresolved
- block 1: 8̈ (l.2); c(h)arte/dis?che group (l.2); barred ⊖ at start of l.3; ƈ with bar (l.5, a person, sender from Rouen?);
  ẇ. and ẇ: (l.6); ξ̣ with dot (read as "he"/"him", uncertain); ‖ before "wrote" in l.5 (maybe part of ⊕|).
- block 2: mostly unread; the name in (1), the sign in (2), the words in (3) and the whole last line.

## Crib checks added after the first pass (21 Sept 2026)
- 20 Oct 1562 = Forbes ii 122 (CSP F. 5 no. 880, "a few words in cipher, deciphered"). Plaintext of the passage:
  "[Newhaven] is not so strong as supposed; if it were summer they would attempt to afray our men thence with a
  garden [(2) Xπ6ξL3 = garden, 9πξL = side] of chamillion [(3), first read 'Chatillon'] upon the hill on this side ...
  things begun there by [(4) France] be not perfit." All four passages now read (glyphs + Forbes).
- 4 Nov 1563 = CSP F. 6 no. 1366: "De Montaigne, whose carte of La Rochelle he sends herewith, and under another
  pretence has given him letters to the Lord Robert and Mr. Secretary, to whom he wrote from Rouen of the same matter.
  The other letters he carried himself, and in the feat whereof he wrote, thinks he can do, but this at this time is
  most necessary." The unread group "dis?che" = la Rochelle. Read in full.
- 31 Jan 1562/3 advertisements: not in Forbes, not found in CSP F. 6 (Jan 26-31, Feb 1-5). (2) = "the queen of
  Navarre" (name sign = French King / K. of Navarre, used with 'queen of'). (1) "Monsieur de [?]uy", (3) and the last
  line remain fragments. This is the only part of R9236 not read.

- Second pass on 31 Jan: last line reads '[?] band that [K. of Navarre] and [?day] with …'; context in CSP F. 6 (Warwick, late Jan 1563: 'the bruit of the Queen of Navarre's band'). Still not read.

decipher_E.md

# R9236 (Add MS 4136 f.140): five letters, Oct 1562 - Nov 1563, read with key R9261

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

# Smith to Cecil, Add MS 4136 f.140 (DECODE R9236), early blocks

Image decode/IMG_R9236_I43221_P.jpg. Key: ./KEY.md. Glyph conventions as in ./decipher_A.md.
Blocks 1 and 3 are printed in Forbes, *A Full View* vol. II (./forbes2.txt): 15 Oct 1562 at p.119
(lines ~10613-10622), 15/17 Dec 1562 at pp.237-238 (lines ~20633-20645). The glyph readings agree with
Forbes. I did not find block 2 (20 Oct 1562) in Forbes; it is read from the glyphs only.
[?] = uncertain.

## 1. 15 Oct 1562 (y ~380-770)
Forbes: "I am certaynlie advertised by diverse Scots, that there be divers sent allredie ... to move the queen of
Scots to break with you, and to make war out of hand; and that the mater is now in hand ... that the hostages
which you have shall be convoyed of into Flanders ... secretary Rollet and monsieur Vyllemort."

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | arrow-triangle | Scots |
| 2 | ▷— | Queen of Scots |
| 3 | ‡ 5 L ⊔ θ · and(null) · Ẅ ẏ ·A T̈ m: ẇ6[?] | break with you and to make war (5 for 5. r; the last sign, a dotted w, looks like a war code word) |
| 4 | ·T T. ω 9 3 ⊔ X L 8 / :W ẏ ·h ·ſ 6: ς̣ξ | that the hostages [9/3 for o/s/t, dots dropped] which you have shall be [6: for b:?] convoyed (ς̣ = C. convoy + ξ d) |
| 5 | ▥ | Flanders |
| 6 | ġ ⊔ 6 9: · 5. 6 ξ} 5 3: · ⊓ 2 ξ · ⊢̈ y: / 7: 9 {ξ ω L ⊢ 7 6. 3: | secretary[?] Rollet and Monsieur Vyllemort (first word read from Forbes, glyphs do not spell it clearly; the last word spells "…l e m o r t") |

Not yet explained: line 3 "7:9{ξωL⊢76.3:" was taken as the second line of (6), Vyllemort (the ending "L⊢76.3:" = e-m-o-r-t, with ⊢ missing its dots); the opening "7:9{ξω" is not letter-perfect for "Vyll".
Forbes has "out of hand" after "war", and no glyphs stand for it.

## 2. 20 Oct 1562 (y ~1100-1400)
| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | —⊙ | Newhaven |
| 2 | X π 6 ξ L 3 / 9 π ξ L | g-i-o-d-e-n / p-i-d-e. Unresolved; perhaps "Gideon[?] side[?]" (9 for 9. s) |
| 3 | Dover(null) · α ω ⊔ 3 π ξ} ⊥⊥ 6 3 · m a ‡ γ · ⅊⅊8[?] · T̈ · ω π ξ · ⟨blot⟩ 6 3 · T̈ | Chatillon[?] (c-h-a-n[t]-i-ll-i-o-n) … "mabc" [?] … to … hid[e][?] … [?]on … to. Only "Chatillon" is fairly firm; the rest is unresolved (a blot covers one sign) |
| 4 | +○ | French / France |

## 3. 15/17 Dec 1562 (pasted slip, y ~1770-2500)
Forbes (15 Dec): "Methincks, there is no good to be done that way for Calais ... I se, you must hold ferme and
fast: Frenchmen will com to reason, but compellid. Ye must give marvelous warning to My Lorde of Warwick to
trust no Frenchman, of what religion soever he be; and that he take marvelous hid of treason, for in that is all
their trust: for whither thei fight with the prince of Conde, which I do not beleve, or they agree within
themselfes, which is most to be suspectid; as sone as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force."

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ⊙— | Calais |
| 2 | and yf(nulls) ·1 8.L ẏ ⊢̈ 5:9.2: ω7}ξ / □L5.⊢̈L ·A □⊓9. +○ M. ℭ γ7⊢̈ T̈ ξ̣ ‡5:2: γ7⊢̈9L}}πξ London(null) | I see, you must hold ferme and fast: Frenchmen will come to r[eason] (ξ̣, a single sign read as the reason code) but compellid |
| 3 | bycause(null) T̈ ⊄̣[trust] n̅ +○ M. .o ẁ 6.L}πXπ72 ◌ ē ξ b: hastings(null) | to trust no Frenchman of what religion so ever he be |
| 3a | F. Lyn(null) | treason[?] (F. taken as the treason code word, following Forbes; the key image does not confirm it) |
| 4 | f ·1 ·T ʃ A: T·7· | for in that is all their trust (the last two signs read as their + trust [?]) |
| 5 | ⊖| :W 1 ẟ n̲ ‡:? }L7:L for(null) | the Prince of Condé, which I do not believe |
| 6 | :W ʃ o: T̈ b: 10.6:9.8Lγ2:πξ / a. b[?]. a. ē T̈ b: ⊔‡}L T̈[they] γ̈ 2:ωπ3ωL7. Ẅ[will] A: T6. ⊞77.γL and(null) | which is most to be suspected; as soon as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force |

## Unresolved
- Block 1: the dotted w at the end of (3) is taken as "war"; the first word of (6) (ġ⊔69:) is read as secretary only because of Forbes.
- Block 2: passage (2), and all of (3) except Chatillon[?]. The letter was not found in Forbes.
- Block 3: "F." is read as treason and "T·7·" as their trust from Forbes; ⊞ (boxed f) = f in "force".
# Add MS 4136 f.140 (DECODE R9236): late blocks

Conventions as in ./decipher_A.md. Clear-English nulls (don, and, lyn, poole, boston, dover, garnesey) are left out.
New sign: `‖` = i (it fits in given, necessarie, is, in, I). The key row for `‖` is headed "ll"(?), but in this copy it reads as i.

no22_reading.md

<!-- Reading of no. 22, built 22 Oct 2026 by Claude Opus 5.5 (batch b03). The seven-line cipher block of Add MS 4136 f. 140 (DECODE R9236; image fetched with the project cookie) re-read on the image with Smith's key R9261 (decipher_E.md late block 1 corrected; no01-no23_reread_log.md). Corrections: c-a-r-t-e of l-a r-o-c-h-e-ll-e (the line-initial sign is the key's ll); ‖ = i; ɛ. = him and ɛ̇ = he in this copy; Ꮚ̄ = whom (key). Setting (CSP Foreign vi no. 1366, which agrees throughout): "De Montaigne, whose carte of La Rochelle he sends herewith, and under another pretence has given him letters to the Lord Robert and Mr. Secretary, to whom he wrote from Rouen of the same matter. The other letters he carried himself, and in the feat whereof he wrote, thinks he can do, but this at this time is most necessary." The sign before "here with" is written :b (by), where the sense and the calendar want "sent"; "whom" (a barred C-like code sign) and "other" (ẅ.) rest on the calendar as well as the key list. Spelling (v6, 23 Oct 2026): the figures', as Smith's key spells them (no01-no23_reread_log.md, decipher_*.md, no01-no23_corrections.tsv); the modernised spelling of 22 Oct restored by hand where the figures spell a word (code signs for names, places and titles carry no spelling and keep the key's gloss). No word was changed. Modern spelling is only in the English file. -->

# No. 22, Sir Thomas Smith to the Queen, 4 November 1563

De Montaigne, that I [?do] speake of, whose carte of La Rochelle I have [?sent] here with; and under an other pretence I
have given him letters to my Lord Robert and Mr Secretarie, and to [?whom] from Rouen I wrote of the same matter. The
[?other] letters he carried himselfe; and in that feate whereof I wrote somwhat, I thinke he can do; but this at this tyme
is most necessarie.
Translation

no22_en.md

<!-- English of no22_reading.md. -->

# No. 22, Smith to Queen Elizabeth, 4 November 1563 — English

De Montaigne, of whom I [?do] speak, whose map of La Rochelle I have [?sent] herewith; and under another pretext I have
given him letters to my Lord Robert (Dudley) and Mr Secretary (Cecil), to whom I wrote from Rouen about the same
matter. The other letters he carried himself; and in the undertaking I wrote of, I think he can succeed; but this, at
this time, is most necessary.

No. 23, Smith advertisements from France, 31 Jan 1562/3 (R9236, f. 140) decoded, not read

Sir Thomas Smith advertisements from France, 31 Jan 1562/3: the ciphered passages as Patrick Forbes copied them into his deciphering file (Add MS 4136), deciphered with Smith's key R9261 (passage (2) on the queen of Navarre; the rest fragments; checked against not found in Forbes or CSP F. 6). In decipher_E.md, late block 2: glyphs and plaintext per passage.

A mechanical decode with holes in most sentences: shown so that the transcription and the key can be checked and improved, not as a reading.

Transcription

| 6 | T. ẇ.[?] l̲10. ξ̣[?] γ⊓6.шLξ ξ. 8.L⊽□4 .A ‖4 ·T □L⊓3:4 ẇ:[?] .o ‖ | that [?] letters [he?] carried himselfe, and in that feate [whereof?] I |
| 7 | ʢ6.53:L 10.7⊢̈ ʢω⊓4: ‖ 4:ωπγθ4 ξ̣[?] γ⊓4 ξ̣[?] ‡5:4: 2:ωπ9. / ⊓4: 3:ϖπ10. 2:10:⊢̈4 ‖9. ⊢̈69.4: 2LγL9.8.⊓6.‖4 | wrote somwhat, I thinke [he?] can do; but this at this tyme is most necessarie |

Running text: "...de Montaign[e] is that I [?] speake of, whose [c(h)arte?] of [dispatch?] [?] I have by here with and under
another pretence. I have given [him] letters to my Lord Robert and Mr Secretarie, and to [?] from Rouen [?] wrote of that same
matter, that [?] letters [he] carried himselfe; and in that feate [whereof] I wrote somwhat I thinke [he] can do. But this at
this tyme is most necessarie."

## Block 2: advertisements from France, 31 Jan 1562 (upright, below the signature)

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | o:(Monsieur) ξL ſ.[?]6:9: | Monsieur de [L?]uy[?] (name unread) |
| 2 | :q.(queen) .o ♁-cross[?] | the queen of [Navarre?] |
| 3 | :q. ξ̈(hath) m̲[?] L8T̈[?] 7.π5:3: 8̈[?] | the queen hath [?] ... (unread) |
| last | ξ ‡⊓2ξ T. ⊕ .a γ⊓9: Ẅ ξ ω. ξ ⊓10..H | [I?] band[?] that Rouen as [?] with ... (unread) |

## Unresolved
- block 1: 8̈ (l.2); c(h)arte/dis?che group (l.2); barred ⊖ at start of l.3; ƈ with bar (l.5, a person, sender from Rouen?);
  ẇ. and ẇ: (l.6); ξ̣ with dot (read as "he"/"him", uncertain); ‖ before "wrote" in l.5 (maybe part of ⊕|).
- block 2: mostly unread; the name in (1), the sign in (2), the words in (3) and the whole last line.

## Crib checks added after the first pass (21 Sept 2026)
- 20 Oct 1562 = Forbes ii 122 (CSP F. 5 no. 880, "a few words in cipher, deciphered"). Plaintext of the passage:
  "[Newhaven] is not so strong as supposed; if it were summer they would attempt to afray our men thence with a
  garden [(2) Xπ6ξL3 = garden, 9πξL = side] of chamillion [(3), first read 'Chatillon'] upon the hill on this side ...
  things begun there by [(4) France] be not perfit." All four passages now read (glyphs + Forbes).
- 4 Nov 1563 = CSP F. 6 no. 1366: "De Montaigne, whose carte of La Rochelle he sends herewith, and under another
  pretence has given him letters to the Lord Robert and Mr. Secretary, to whom he wrote from Rouen of the same matter.
  The other letters he carried himself, and in the feat whereof he wrote, thinks he can do, but this at this time is
  most necessary." The unread group "dis?che" = la Rochelle. Read in full.
- 31 Jan 1562/3 advertisements: not in Forbes, not found in CSP F. 6 (Jan 26-31, Feb 1-5). (2) = "the queen of
  Navarre" (name sign = French King / K. of Navarre, used with 'queen of'). (1) "Monsieur de [?]uy", (3) and the last
  line remain fragments. This is the only part of R9236 not read.

- Second pass on 31 Jan: last line reads '[?] band that [K. of Navarre] and [?day] with …'; context in CSP F. 6 (Warwick, late Jan 1563: 'the bruit of the Queen of Navarre's band'). Still not read.

decipher_E.md

# R9236 (Add MS 4136 f.140): five letters, Oct 1562 - Nov 1563, read with key R9261

> 22 Oct 2026 (re-read, batch b03): labels and values corrected passage by passage in no01-no23_corrections.tsv; the readings are in no<NN>_reading.md. Where this file and the tsv differ, the tsv holds.

# Smith to Cecil, Add MS 4136 f.140 (DECODE R9236), early blocks

Image decode/IMG_R9236_I43221_P.jpg. Key: ./KEY.md. Glyph conventions as in ./decipher_A.md.
Blocks 1 and 3 are printed in Forbes, *A Full View* vol. II (./forbes2.txt): 15 Oct 1562 at p.119
(lines ~10613-10622), 15/17 Dec 1562 at pp.237-238 (lines ~20633-20645). The glyph readings agree with
Forbes. I did not find block 2 (20 Oct 1562) in Forbes; it is read from the glyphs only.
[?] = uncertain.

## 1. 15 Oct 1562 (y ~380-770)
Forbes: "I am certaynlie advertised by diverse Scots, that there be divers sent allredie ... to move the queen of
Scots to break with you, and to make war out of hand; and that the mater is now in hand ... that the hostages
which you have shall be convoyed of into Flanders ... secretary Rollet and monsieur Vyllemort."

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | arrow-triangle | Scots |
| 2 | ▷— | Queen of Scots |
| 3 | ‡ 5 L ⊔ θ · and(null) · Ẅ ẏ ·A T̈ m: ẇ6[?] | break with you and to make war (5 for 5. r; the last sign, a dotted w, looks like a war code word) |
| 4 | ·T T. ω 9 3 ⊔ X L 8 / :W ẏ ·h ·ſ 6: ς̣ξ | that the hostages [9/3 for o/s/t, dots dropped] which you have shall be [6: for b:?] convoyed (ς̣ = C. convoy + ξ d) |
| 5 | ▥ | Flanders |
| 6 | ġ ⊔ 6 9: · 5. 6 ξ} 5 3: · ⊓ 2 ξ · ⊢̈ y: / 7: 9 {ξ ω L ⊢ 7 6. 3: | secretary[?] Rollet and Monsieur Vyllemort (first word read from Forbes, glyphs do not spell it clearly; the last word spells "…l e m o r t") |

Not yet explained: line 3 "7:9{ξωL⊢76.3:" was taken as the second line of (6), Vyllemort (the ending "L⊢76.3:" = e-m-o-r-t, with ⊢ missing its dots); the opening "7:9{ξω" is not letter-perfect for "Vyll".
Forbes has "out of hand" after "war", and no glyphs stand for it.

## 2. 20 Oct 1562 (y ~1100-1400)
| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | —⊙ | Newhaven |
| 2 | X π 6 ξ L 3 / 9 π ξ L | g-i-o-d-e-n / p-i-d-e. Unresolved; perhaps "Gideon[?] side[?]" (9 for 9. s) |
| 3 | Dover(null) · α ω ⊔ 3 π ξ} ⊥⊥ 6 3 · m a ‡ γ · ⅊⅊8[?] · T̈ · ω π ξ · ⟨blot⟩ 6 3 · T̈ | Chatillon[?] (c-h-a-n[t]-i-ll-i-o-n) … "mabc" [?] … to … hid[e][?] … [?]on … to. Only "Chatillon" is fairly firm; the rest is unresolved (a blot covers one sign) |
| 4 | +○ | French / France |

## 3. 15/17 Dec 1562 (pasted slip, y ~1770-2500)
Forbes (15 Dec): "Methincks, there is no good to be done that way for Calais ... I se, you must hold ferme and
fast: Frenchmen will com to reason, but compellid. Ye must give marvelous warning to My Lorde of Warwick to
trust no Frenchman, of what religion soever he be; and that he take marvelous hid of treason, for in that is all
their trust: for whither thei fight with the prince of Conde, which I do not beleve, or they agree within
themselfes, which is most to be suspectid; as sone as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force."

| # | signs | plaintext |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | ⊙— | Calais |
| 2 | and yf(nulls) ·1 8.L ẏ ⊢̈ 5:9.2: ω7}ξ / □L5.⊢̈L ·A □⊓9. +○ M. ℭ γ7⊢̈ T̈ ξ̣ ‡5:2: γ7⊢̈9L}}πξ London(null) | I see, you must hold ferme and fast: Frenchmen will come to r[eason] (ξ̣, a single sign read as the reason code) but compellid |
| 3 | bycause(null) T̈ ⊄̣[trust] n̅ +○ M. .o ẁ 6.L}πXπ72 ◌ ē ξ b: hastings(null) | to trust no Frenchman of what religion so ever he be |
| 3a | F. Lyn(null) | treason[?] (F. taken as the treason code word, following Forbes; the key image does not confirm it) |
| 4 | f ·1 ·T ʃ A: T·7· | for in that is all their trust (the last two signs read as their + trust [?]) |
| 5 | ⊖| :W 1 ẟ n̲ ‡:? }L7:L for(null) | the Prince of Condé, which I do not believe |
| 6 | :W ʃ o: T̈ b: 10.6:9.8Lγ2:πξ / a. b[?]. a. ē T̈ b: ⊔‡}L T̈[they] γ̈ 2:ωπ3ωL7. Ẅ[will] A: T6. ⊞77.γL and(null) | which is most to be suspected; as soon as ever they be able, they will thither will all their force |

## Unresolved
- Block 1: the dotted w at the end of (3) is taken as "war"; the first word of (6) (ġ⊔69:) is read as secretary only because of Forbes.
- Block 2: passage (2), and all of (3) except Chatillon[?]. The letter was not found in Forbes.
- Block 3: "F." is read as treason and "T·7·" as their trust from Forbes; ⊞ (boxed f) = f in "force".
# Add MS 4136 f.140 (DECODE R9236): late blocks

Conventions as in ./decipher_A.md. Clear-English nulls (don, and, lyn, poole, boston, dover, garnesey) are left out.
New sign: `‖` = i (it fits in given, necessarie, is, in, I). The key row for `‖` is headed "ll"(?), but in this copy it reads as i.

| 3 | ⊖̄4[?] π ω⊓6:4 :b ϖL6.4 Ẅ .A 5:4ξL7. ⊓4 74:ωL6. | [?] I have by here with and under an other |
